# Complete supplied source

# Opinion brief and source

SYNTHETIC EVALUATION MATERIAL. Fictional policy debate, not legal advice or a real municipal process. Source language: Swedish.

## Brief

Skriv en debattartikel för invånarna i fiktiva Lervik. Avsändare är Sanna Ek, talesperson för föreningen Öppna beslut. Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb. En tydlig, sakligt skarp text på ungefär 400 ord. Markdown med orienterande rubriker. Ingen kommersiell CTA.

## Underlag

Kommunens pilotrapport Bokning av föreningslokaler, 8 april 2026, gäller ett åtta veckor långt försök i två lokaler. Under försöket gjordes 96 bokningar via webb och 24 via telefon. Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer. Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana. Telefonbokningarna kan därför inte användas för att säga hur stor andel av invånarna som inte kan boka digitalt.

Tjänsteutlåtandet inför kommunstyrelsens möte den 18 juni föreslår att telefonbokning tas bort för alla sju lokaler från september. Motivet i utlåtandet är att personalen ska slippa föra in uppgifter i två flöden. Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna. Förvaltningens verkliga invändning mot att behålla båda kanalerna är dubbel administration, inte att telefonanvändare är lata eller dyra.

Öppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar. Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg och be användarna frivilligt ange varför de väljer telefon eller webb. Föreningen gör inget anspråk på att ha finansierat eller kostnadsberäknat försöket. Kostnaden behöver kommunstyrelsen ta ställning till. Därefter kan den avgöra om en kanal ska tas bort, ändras eller behållas.

Sanna Eks ståndpunkt: ”Att en bokning går via telefon är inte ett bevis för att någon inte kan använda webben. Det är ett skäl att ta reda på varför telefonen fortfarande används.” Detta får citeras eller refereras. Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna. Hon motsätter sig inte digital bokning.

## Boundaries / judging

Inga lagkrav, protester, diskrimineringsfall, partipolitiska motiv eller besparingar är belagda. Siffror ska tillskrivas rapporten där de bär argumentet. Författaren ska kunna kritisera beslutsunderlaget skarpt utan falska motiv eller tandlös neutralisering. Slutet ska namnge kommunstyrelsens möjliga beslut, med kostnadsosäkerheten bevarad.

# Delivered user response

The draft is delivered with a known source-support defect: “familiarity with digital technology” can include theoretical knowledge; the source’s “digital vana” concerns being accustomed to using it. The checker proposes “how accustomed users are to using digital technology”.

Both comparisons have run. The [write skill](/private/var/folders/cs/vgp_x_353zd9frkjpysp7zdw0000gn/T/kntnt-native-session-r95dqkak/work/.agents/skills/write/SKILL.md) requires “never reopen the prose after its final source comparison”, so the compared text is preserved below. Resolved: opinion, British English, no technique, as the opinion genre specifies. Trial costs remain unspecified. No files were retained.

```markdown
---
kntnt:
  genre: opinion
  technique: none
  language: en_GB
---

# Lervik should test before ending telephone bookings

Booking totals in Lervik’s pilot report do not measure residents’ ability to book online. The municipal executive board must weigh staff costs against the value of telephone access, yet the documents contain neither a time measurement nor a calculation of financial savings.

By Sanna Ek, spokesperson for Öppna beslut

The administrative report for Lervik’s municipal executive board meeting on 18 June proposes removing telephone booking from all seven venues for associations from September. I do not oppose digital booking. But on behalf of Öppna beslut, I urge the board to postpone a permanent switch and test telephone and web booking together for six months.

## Bookings tell us too little about users

The municipality’s pilot report, dated 8 April 2026, records 96 web bookings and 24 telephone bookings during an eight-week trial at two venues. Those are bookings, not unique people. The report measures neither age nor functional ability nor familiarity with digital technology.

So the telephone figure cannot tell us what proportion of Lervik’s residents cannot book online. Nor does choosing the telephone prove an inability to use the web. It gives us a reason to ask why that route is still used.

## Staff time needs a fair comparison

The administration’s objection to keeping both routes is the administrative work across two channels: staff enter information in two workflows. That concern deserves to be taken seriously. But the documents provide no measurement of the time involved or calculation of financial savings.

Removing a route before establishing its value to users and the work it requires leaves the board without the comparison it needs. Administrative simplicity is a reason to investigate the balance, not a substitute for doing so.

## A wider trial can inform the choice

Öppna beslut proposes keeping both booking routes for six months at all seven venues. During that trial, the administration should record the time spent on each route and ask users to say voluntarily why they choose telephone or web booking.

We make no claim to have financed or costed that trial. The executive board must consider its cost. Asking for better evidence does not remove the need to decide what gathering it is worth.

## Evidence should guide the final booking decision

Lervik’s municipal executive board should now postpone the permanent switch and agree a six-month trial of both routes, with its cost explicitly considered. After that, it can decide whether to remove, change or retain a booking channel.
```
# Extracted Text Artifact

---
kntnt:

  technique: none
  language: en_GB

# Lervik should test before ending telephone bookings

Booking totals in Lervik’s pilot report do not measure residents’ ability to book online. The municipal executive board must weigh staff costs against the value of telephone access, yet the documents contain neither a time measurement nor a calculation of financial savings.

By Sanna Ek, spokesperson for Öppna beslut

The administrative report for Lervik’s municipal executive board meeting on 18 June proposes removing telephone booking from all seven venues for associations from September. I do not oppose digital booking. But on behalf of Öppna beslut, I urge the board to postpone a permanent switch and test telephone and web booking together for six months.

## Bookings tell us too little about users

The municipality’s pilot report, dated 8 April 2026, records 96 web bookings and 24 telephone bookings during an eight-week trial at two venues. Those are bookings, not unique people. The report measures neither age nor functional ability nor familiarity with digital technology.

So the telephone figure cannot tell us what proportion of Lervik’s residents cannot book online. Nor does choosing the telephone prove an inability to use the web. It gives us a reason to ask why that route is still used.

## Staff time needs a fair comparison

The administration’s objection to keeping both routes is the administrative work across two channels: staff enter information in two workflows. That concern deserves to be taken seriously. But the documents provide no measurement of the time involved or calculation of financial savings.

Removing a route before establishing its value to users and the work it requires leaves the board without the comparison it needs. Administrative simplicity is a reason to investigate the balance, not a substitute for doing so.

## A wider trial can inform the choice

Öppna beslut proposes keeping both booking routes for six months at all seven venues. During that trial, the administration should record the time spent on each route and ask users to say voluntarily why they choose telephone or web booking.

We make no claim to have financed or costed that trial. The executive board must consider its cost. Asking for better evidence does not remove the need to decide what gathering it is worth.

## Evidence should guide the final booking decision

Lervik’s municipal executive board should now postpone the permanent switch and agree a six-month trial of both routes, with its cost explicitly considered. After that, it can decide whether to remove, change or retain a booking channel.

# Lervik should test before ending telephone bookings

Booking totals in Lervik’s pilot report do not measure residents’ ability to book online. The municipal executive board must weigh staff costs against the value of telephone access, yet the documents contain neither a time measurement nor a calculation of financial savings.

By Sanna Ek, spokesperson for Öppna beslut

The officer’s report for Lervik’s municipal executive board meeting on 18 June proposes removing telephone booking from all seven venues for local associations from September. I do not oppose digital booking. But on behalf of Öppna beslut, I urge the board to postpone a permanent switch and test telephone and web booking together for six months.

## Bookings tell us too little about users

The municipality’s pilot report, dated 8 April 2026, records 96 web bookings and 24 telephone bookings during an eight-week trial at two venues. Those are bookings, not unique people. The report measures neither age nor functional ability nor familiarity with digital technology.

So the telephone figure cannot tell us what proportion of Lervik’s residents cannot book online. Nor does choosing the telephone prove an inability to use the web. It gives us a reason to ask why that route is still used.

## Staff time needs a fair comparison

The administration’s objection to keeping both routes is the administrative work across two channels: staff enter information in two workflows. That concern deserves to be taken seriously. But the documents provide no measurement of the time involved or calculation of financial savings.

Removing a route before establishing its value to users and the work it requires leaves the board without the comparison it needs. Administrative simplicity is a reason to investigate the balance, not a substitute for doing so.

## A wider trial can inform the choice

Öppna beslut proposes keeping both booking routes for six months at all seven venues. During that trial, the administration should record the time spent on each route and ask users to say voluntarily why they choose telephone or web booking.

We make no claim to have financed or costed that trial. The executive board must consider its cost. Asking for better evidence does not remove the need to decide what gathering it is worth.

## The board can make its lasting choice later

Lervik’s municipal executive board should now postpone the permanent switch and agree a six-month trial of both routes, with its cost explicitly considered. After that, it can decide whether to remove, change or retain a booking channel.

# Complete source and quotation comparison

Examined the full Swedish source.md and the full British English draft.md, including all headline, standfirst, byline, subheadings and body passages. The only factual source is the supplied fictional Lervik brief. No direct speech quotation occurs in the draft. No sources or draft were changed.

## Claim accounting

For each item below, quoted source wording retains the qualifications needed for the comparison. Authorial arguments are identified separately from claims about facts.

1. **Headline: “Lervik should test before ending telephone bookings”.**
   Source: “Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb.”
   This is the commissioned recommendation. The municipal place name stands for its decision-making body. “Before” retains the order; the body supplies the trial length and permanent-change qualification. It does not say telephone booking has already ended. Supported advocacy.

2. **Standfirst: “Booking totals in Lervik’s pilot report do not measure residents’ ability to book online.”**
   Source: “Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer. Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana. Telefonbokningarna kan därför inte användas för att säga hur stor andel av invånarna som inte kan boka digitalt.”
   The negative concerns what booking totals measure, not every conceivable item elsewhere in the report. It does not assert that residents can book online or that callers have no difficulties. Supported explanation of the count’s limits.

3. **Standfirst: “The municipal executive board must weigh staff costs against the value of telephone access”.**
   Source: “Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna.”
   Context: “Att en bokning går via telefon är inte ett bevis för att någon inte kan använda webben. Det är ett skäl att ta reda på varför telefonen fortfarande används.”
   The byline and first-person body preserve Sanna’s ownership of this position. This is advocacy, not an asserted legal duty. Focusing user value on the route proposed for removal is supported argument about that proposal, not an invented measured benefit. Staff costs renders “arbetskostnaden”; the board is the decision-maker identified in the brief.

4. **Standfirst: “yet the documents contain neither a time measurement nor a calculation of financial savings.”**
   Source: “Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna.”
   The documentary scope and both absences match. “Financial savings” is idiomatic for the supplied economic savings calculation in this staff-cost context. No claim that savings are impossible is added.

5. **Byline: “By Sanna Ek, spokesperson for Öppna beslut”.**
   Source: “Avsändare är Sanna Ek, talesperson för föreningen Öppna beslut.”
   Author and role match; the byline does not identify the user. English “spokesperson” asserts no natural gender.

6. **Opening: “The officer’s report for Lervik’s municipal executive board meeting on 18 June”.**
   Source: “Tjänsteutlåtandet inför kommunstyrelsens möte den 18 juni”
   Body, meeting and date match; no meeting year has been introduced. “For” retains the report’s connection with the forthcoming meeting. “The officer’s” adds singular officer provenance not supplied by the document designation. See SS-1.

7. **Opening: “proposes removing telephone booking from all seven venues for local associations from September.”**
   Source: “föreslår att telefonbokning tas bort för alla sju lokaler från september.”
   Defining context: “ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler”; “Kommunens pilotrapport Bokning av föreningslokaler”.
   Proposed status, telephone route, all seven premises and September match. No adopted decision is invented. “Venues” is compatible with the bookable premises in this context. “Local associations” adds a geographical restriction to the associations. See SS-2.

8. **Opening: “I do not oppose digital booking.”**
   Source: “Hon motsätter sig inte digital bokning.”
   The authorised first-person author is Sanna. The stance matches and does not imply support for every online-only proposal.

9. **Opening: “But on behalf of Öppna beslut, I urge the board to postpone a permanent switch and test telephone and web booking together for six months.”**
   Source: “Avsändare är Sanna Ek, talesperson för föreningen Öppna beslut. Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb.”
   Further source: “Öppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar.”
   Representative capacity, requested postponement, permanence, both routes and duration match. The previous sentence identifies the switch. This does not report approval or financing.

10. **Subheading: “Bookings tell us too little about users”.**
    Source: “Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer. Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana. Telefonbokningarna kan därför inte användas för att säga hur stor andel av invånarna som inte kan boka digitalt.”
    “Too little” is the author’s evaluation against the information needed for this decision. It does not characterise the count of 24 as high or low. The following prose specifies the information limits. Supported argument.

11. **Pilot paragraph: “The municipality’s pilot report, dated 8 April 2026, records 96 web bookings and 24 telephone bookings during an eight-week trial at two venues.”**
    Source: “Kommunens pilotrapport Bokning av föreningslokaler, 8 april 2026, gäller ett åtta veckor långt försök i två lokaler. Under försöket gjordes 96 bokningar via webb och 24 via telefon.”
    Report attribution, date, counts, booking unit, channels, eight weeks and two premises match. The figures are explicitly attributed where they support the argument. Omission of the report’s title does not introduce a different title.

12. **“Those are bookings, not unique people.”**
    Source: “Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer.”
    Same unit and exclusion; unique people are not counted or estimated.

13. **“The report measures neither age nor functional ability nor familiarity with digital technology.”**
    Source: “Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana.”
    All three exclusions match. In this user-access context, “functional ability” denotes personal functional capacity, and “familiarity with digital technology” denotes practical digital familiarity. The latter is not read here as academic knowledge about technology. No concrete contextual obstruction, diagnosis or population figure has been added.

14. **“So the telephone figure cannot tell us what proportion of Lervik’s residents cannot book online.”**
    Source: “Telefonbokningarna kan därför inte användas för att säga hur stor andel av invånarna som inte kan boka digitalt.”
    Population scope, inference limit, inability and channel match. Unknown inability has not been converted into proven ability.

15. **“Nor does choosing the telephone prove an inability to use the web.”**
    Source: “Sanna Eks ståndpunkt: ‘Att en bokning går via telefon är inte ett bevis för att någon inte kan använda webben.’”
    The stance is Sanna’s, indirectly rendered without quotation marks. “Choosing” also has support in “varför de väljer telefon eller webb”; it does not assert unconstrained choices or no barriers. No individual’s motive or ability is invented.

16. **“It gives us a reason to ask why that route is still used.”**
    Source: “Det är ett skäl att ta reda på varför telefonen fortfarande används.”
    Telephone use supplies a reason to investigate. Continued use matches; no reason for that use is asserted as known.

17. **Subheading: “Staff time needs a fair comparison”.**
    Source: “Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg”; “Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna.”
    Supported authorial evaluation of the proposed comparison, without asserting an existing measured comparison or quantified unfairness.

18. **“The administration’s objection to keeping both routes is the administrative work across two channels: staff enter information in two workflows.”**
    Source: “Motivet i utlåtandet är att personalen ska slippa föra in uppgifter i två flöden. Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna. Förvaltningens verkliga invändning mot att behålla båda kanalerna är dubbel administration, inte att telefonanvändare är lata eller dyra.”
    Actual supplied objection and operational explanation match. Avoiding entry in two workflows supports the work being avoided. There is no time amount, cost amount, laziness, party motive or causal savings claim.

19. **“That concern deserves to be taken seriously.”**
    Source: “Förvaltningens verkliga invändning mot att behålla båda kanalerna är dubbel administration”; “Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna.”
    The author assesses a supported concern; no view is attributed to a new person. Supported argument.

20. **“But the documents provide no measurement of the time involved or calculation of financial savings.”**
    Source: “Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna.”
    Same documentary scope and absences. The preceding workflow clause settles “time involved”. No absence outside the documents is claimed.

21. **“Removing a route before establishing its value to users and the work it requires leaves the board without the comparison it needs.”**
    Source: “Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna.”
    Factual premise: “Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna.”
    Proposed investigation: “Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg och be användarna frivilligt ange varför de väljer telefon eller webb.”
    This is conditional authorial reasoning about removal before the relevant comparison. It does not report an actual removal, a quantified causal effect or the absence of every form of user information everywhere.

22. **“Administrative simplicity is a reason to investigate the balance, not a substitute for doing so.”**
    Source: “Motivet i utlåtandet är att personalen ska slippa föra in uppgifter i två flöden”; “Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna.”
    Characterising avoidance of double administration as an aim of simplicity is supported. The remaining clause is Sanna’s argument, not a measured technical effect or consensus.

23. **Subheading: “A wider trial can inform the choice”.**
    Source: “ett åtta veckor långt försök i två lokaler”; “Öppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar. Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg och be användarna frivilligt ange varför de väljer telefon eller webb”; “Därefter kan den avgöra om en kanal ska tas bort, ändras eller behållas.”
    “Wider” has a comparison: seven premises rather than two, plus a longer period. “Can inform” states the proposed evidence-gathering purpose; it guarantees no outcome.

24. **“Öppna beslut proposes keeping both booking routes for six months at all seven venues.”**
    Source: “Öppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar.”
    Proposer, proposed status, two routes, duration and coverage match. Subheading and next sentence retain trial status.

25. **“During that trial, the administration should record the time spent on each route and ask users to say voluntarily why they choose telephone or web booking.”**
    Source: “Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg och be användarna frivilligt ange varför de väljer telefon eller webb.”
    Actor, proposed trial, measurement per route, voluntary user disclosure and options match. “Should” is recommendation, not a report of an adopted order. No additional user variables are introduced.

26. **“We make no claim to have financed or costed that trial.”**
    Source: “Föreningen gör inget anspråk på att ha finansierat eller kostnadsberäknat försöket.”
    The institutional speaker is the preceding Öppna beslut. No claim of financing or costing remains distinct from knowing that neither happened. British “costed” means having calculated costs. Supported.

27. **“The executive board must consider its cost.”**
    Source: “Kostnaden behöver kommunstyrelsen ta ställning till.”
    Body and trial cost match. “Consider” is less explicit in isolation than taking a position, but the next sentence supplies deciding what evidence-gathering is worth and the final recommendation puts cost into the board’s trial decision. In context the cost judgement and uncertainty are preserved.

28. **“Asking for better evidence does not remove the need to decide what gathering it is worth.”**
    Source: “Föreningen gör inget anspråk på att ha finansierat eller kostnadsberäknat försöket. Kostnaden behöver kommunstyrelsen ta ställning till.”
    Supported authorial reflection about cost responsibility. No amount, available budget or affordability conclusion is given.

29. **Subheading: “Evidence should guide the final booking decision”.**
    Source: “Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb”; “Därefter kan den avgöra om en kanal ska tas bort, ändras eller behållas.”
    Supported commissioned position. “Final” distinguishes permanent channel choice from trial; it does not assert legal irreversibility.

30. **“Lervik’s municipal executive board should now postpone the permanent switch and agree a six-month trial of both routes, with its cost explicitly considered.”**
    Source: “Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb”; “Öppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar”; “Föreningen gör inget anspråk på att ha finansierat eller kostnadsberäknat försöket. Kostnaden behöver kommunstyrelsen ta ställning till.”
    Actor, recommendation, permanence, period and two routes match. “Now” is advocated timing, not an event. “Agree” is requested, not reported approval. Explicit cost consideration plus the preceding no-claim statement preserves uncertainty.

31. **“After that, it can decide whether to remove, change or retain a booking channel.”**
    Source: “Kostnaden behöver kommunstyrelsen ta ställning till. Därefter kan den avgöra om en kanal ska tas bort, ändras eller behållas.”
    The board, post-trial and cost-consideration sequence, modality and three possible decisions match. Retention is not presented as the only acceptable result.

## Separate pronoun accounting

- “I” in the two opening uses: first-person singular, natural gender unspecified. Authorship and the position are supplied. Source “Hon” is third-person singular and naturally female; the authorised authorial change adds no gender claim.
- “Those” in “Those are bookings”: plural demonstrative, natural gender unspecified; the supplied counts and “bokningar” establish plurality.
- “us” in the first subheading, population-inference sentence and investigation sentence: first-person plural, natural gender unspecified; inclusive author/reader rhetoric for an article to residents. It neither counts a population nor attributes Sanna’s view to every resident.
- “It” in “It gives us a reason”: third-person singular, natural gender unspecified, a reference to telephone use as a fact. Swedish “Det” likewise denotes that fact.
- “that route”, “both routes”, “each route” and “both booking routes”: singular or distributed/plural as stated, natural gender unspecified; the source expressly supplies telephone and web routes.
- “That concern”: singular demonstrative, natural gender unspecified; the supplied double-administration objection.
- “its” in “its value to users” and “it” in “the work it requires”: third-person singular, natural gender unspecified; a hypothetical route in authorial reasoning. They add no measured value or work amount.
- “it” in “the comparison it needs”: third-person singular, natural gender unspecified; the board in Sanna’s argument. It does not assert that the board itself has publicly adopted her assessment.
- “doing so”: refers to investigating the balance; no person, number or natural-gender claim.
- “they” in “why they choose”: third-person plural, natural gender unspecified; source “användarna” and “de väljer” establish plural users without male/female composition.
- “We” in “We make no claim”: first-person plural, natural gender unspecified; organisational voice through its spokesperson. Source “Föreningen” supplies the asserting party, not a specific count of authors, staff or participants.
- “its” in the two trial-cost passages: third-person singular, natural gender unspecified; source “Kostnaden” in the trial paragraph supplies the trial-cost relationship.
- “it” in “gathering it”: third-person singular, natural gender unspecified; evidence in the author’s cost reflection.
- “that” in “After that”: singular demonstrative, natural gender unspecified; the proposed trial/consideration sequence.
- Final “it can decide”: third-person singular, natural gender unspecified; source “den” refers to “kommunstyrelsen”. Swedish grammatical common gender does not assert a human’s natural gender.

No additional human gender, personal experience, individual user or speaker is introduced through these pronouns.

## Source-support findings

### SS-1: singular officer provenance

Draft: “The officer’s report for Lervik’s municipal executive board meeting on 18 June”.

Exact source: “Tjänsteutlåtandet inför kommunstyrelsens möte den 18 juni”.

The source names an administrative document, without a singular officer or its authorship. The draft’s singular possessive adds that provenance.

Two-direction test:
- A municipal department could issue the tjänsteutlåtande jointly through several officials, with no single officer owning or authoring it. The supplied material names the administration and document, but contains no statement excluding that case. The source’s designation holds; the singular officer provenance does not.
- Conversely, a single officer could produce a supporting report for the meeting that is not the formal tjänsteutlåtande. The draft’s generic description could hold while the specific source-document designation does not. No supplied statement equates every officer report with that formal document.

Smallest supported repair: replace “The officer’s report” with “The administrative report”. Preserve its meeting, dates, proposal and other substantive details.

### SS-2: geographical restriction on associations

Draft: “all seven venues for local associations”.

Exact source: “alla sju lokaler”; the premises are defined by “bokning av föreningslokaler”.

The source does not restrict associations to being locally based. Local audience and municipal setting do not supply that restriction.

Two-direction test:
- A nationwide association could use one of these association premises. The supplied “föreningslokaler” description remains true, but the restriction to “local associations” need not hold. No supplied statement excludes national associations or their bookings.
- Every venue for local associations is also a venue for associations; there is no reverse counterexample on this restriction. The asymmetry shows that the draft narrows the source.

Smallest supported repair: delete “local”: “all seven venues for associations”.

Both findings concern source support in translated descriptive prose, rather than translation of quoted speech.

## Quotation and boundary results

There are no direct speech quotations, so no quotation-translation idiom or distinctive-voice findings. Sanna’s indirectly rendered supplied statement preserves its meaning and attribution.

No unsupported legal requirement, protest, discrimination event, party motive, savings amount, causal saving effect, telephone-user motive or personal experience appears. Figures remain attributed to the pilot report. The no-financing/no-costing-claim wording preserves the state of knowledge. Trial cost uncertainty and the board’s three possible final channel decisions remain explicit. The author’s rhetorical evaluations and recommendations are supported as her position rather than externally proved facts.

## Completion status

Comparison complete. Source and draft unchanged. Two unresolved source-support findings remain in the current draft: SS-1, singular officer provenance; SS-2, “local associations”. No quotation-translation findings.

# Captured file version: scratch/write-source-check-phwbhm4c/draft.md

Capture time 1790859471.509242; SHA256 d3eade6d05c12b0db5354a39b947f568bf7c27f4ebf3e245dae01de841522cb7

# Lervik should test before ending telephone bookings

Booking totals in Lervik’s pilot report do not measure residents’ ability to book online. The municipal executive board must weigh staff costs against the value of telephone access, yet the documents contain neither a time measurement nor a calculation of financial savings.

By Sanna Ek, spokesperson for Öppna beslut

The administrative report for Lervik’s municipal executive board meeting on 18 June proposes removing telephone booking from all seven venues for associations from September. I do not oppose digital booking. But on behalf of Öppna beslut, I urge the board to postpone a permanent switch and test telephone and web booking together for six months.

## Bookings tell us too little about users

The municipality’s pilot report, dated 8 April 2026, records 96 web bookings and 24 telephone bookings during an eight-week trial at two venues. Those are bookings, not unique people. The report measures neither age nor functional ability nor familiarity with digital technology.

So the telephone figure cannot tell us what proportion of Lervik’s residents cannot book online. Nor does choosing the telephone prove an inability to use the web. It gives us a reason to ask why that route is still used.

## Staff time needs a fair comparison

The administration’s objection to keeping both routes is the administrative work across two channels: staff enter information in two workflows. That concern deserves to be taken seriously. But the documents provide no measurement of the time involved or calculation of financial savings.

Removing a route before establishing its value to users and the work it requires leaves the board without the comparison it needs. Administrative simplicity is a reason to investigate the balance, not a substitute for doing so.

## A wider trial can inform the choice

Öppna beslut proposes keeping both booking routes for six months at all seven venues. During that trial, the administration should record the time spent on each route and ask users to say voluntarily why they choose telephone or web booking.

We make no claim to have financed or costed that trial. The executive board must consider its cost. Asking for better evidence does not remove the need to decide what gathering it is worth.

## Evidence should guide the final booking decision

Lervik’s municipal executive board should now postpone the permanent switch and agree a six-month trial of both routes, with its cost explicitly considered. After that, it can decide whether to remove, change or retain a booking channel.

| Draft form | Account |
| --- | --- |
| “I” in the two first-person author statements | First person, singular, no natural gender. Referent is Sanna Ek under “Avsändare är Sanna Ek” and the byline. This is the commissioned author’s voice. Source “Hon motsätter sig inte digital bokning” explicitly attributes the position to her; the English first-person form does not reproduce or add a third-person gender assertion. |
| “We” in “We make no claim” | First person, plural, no natural gender. Organisational voice on behalf of Öppna beslut, supported by the spokesperson brief and “Föreningen gör inget anspråk …”. It expresses the association’s position, not an invented assertion about the user or a counted membership. |
| “us” in the heading and explanatory argument | First person, plural, no natural gender. Rhetorical inclusion of author and readers in evaluating evidence. No shared personal experience, actual knowledge, action or measured reader belief is claimed. The source’s intended readers are “invånarna i fiktiva Lervik”. |
| “Those” | Plural demonstrative referring to the 96 and 24 bookings. Source explicitly counts “bokningar, inte unika personer”. It asserts no number of distinct humans. |
| “they” in “why they choose” | Third person, plural, gender-neutral, referring to users asked during the proposed trial. Source has plural “användarna … varför de väljer telefon eller webb”. Both referent and grammatical number are supplied; no natural gender is inferred. |
| “It” in “It gives us a reason” | Third person, singular, nonhuman antecedent: telephone booking/using that route. Matches “Det är ett skäl …”. No natural-gender assertion. |
| “its” in “its value”, “it” in “the work it requires” | Third person, singular, nonhuman booking-route referent. Source discusses the retained telephone/web channels and weighing labour cost against user value. No human or gender assertion. |
| “its cost” in the two trial-cost passages | Third person, singular, nonhuman trial referent. Source explicitly gives “försöket” and “Kostnaden behöver kommunstyrelsen ta ställning till”. No claim that the cost is known. |
| “it” in “it can decide” | Third person, singular, nonhuman institutional referent: municipal executive board. Source “kommunstyrelsen … Därefter kan den avgöra …” supplies that referent and institutional singular. Swedish grammatical common gender in “den” does not become a human natural-gender claim. |

Other demonstratives (“that route”, “That concern”, “that trial”, “After that”) refer respectively to telephone booking, the stated double-administration objection, the proposed six-month trial, and the preceding proposed sequence. Their referents are supplied; they carry no natural gender. “Both” and “each” refer to the two supplied channels, telephone and web. The draft introduces no he/she pronoun or other natural-gender assertion about any person. Sanna’s name alone has not been treated as gender evidence.

## Findings

### F1 — Source-support: measurement exclusion broadened across languages

Draft: “The report measures neither age nor functional ability nor **familiarity with digital technology**.”

Source: “Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller **digital vana**.”

The third term changes what is excluded from measurement. “Digital vana” concerns being accustomed to using digital technology. “Familiarity with digital technology” can also mean knowing about it without practical accustomedness. The source therefore does not establish every measurement absence the English sentence can assert.

**Source-true/draft-false case:** the pilot report could include a test of users’ knowledge of digital tools learned from printed manuals, while measuring neither their practical accustomedness to using those tools, age, nor functional ability. It could still count bookings rather than unique people. Such a report would satisfy the supplied exclusions but would measure a form of familiarity. No supplied statement excludes that case; the source does not describe every other possible measure in the report.

**Reverse-direction test:** a report measuring users’ habitual digital use while omitting a separate knowledge assessment could distinguish practical accustomedness from knowledge-based familiarity. As a case for this actual report, however, it is excluded by the explicit statement “Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana.” The surviving first-direction case is sufficient to establish the draft’s broader absence claim.

**Smallest supported repair:** “The report measures neither age nor functional ability nor **how accustomed users are to using digital technology**.” This retains the measured attributes, certainty and report attribution while narrowing the third term to the supplied one.

No other source-support defect was found. The administration’s explanation remains attributed in its complete sentence; the report’s counts and exclusions remain qualified; the organisation’s absence of a claim is not turned into knowledge of non-occurrence; and the author’s advocacy is not treated as an empirical finding.

## Quotation and translation findings

There is no direct speech quotation in the draft. The supplied quotation is paraphrased with its evidential point and author attribution preserved. There is no translated-quotation finding. F1 concerns the source support of non-quoted prose, rather than quotation voice or idiom. General editorial and mechanical review were not undertaken.

Completion status: complete. Unresolved findings: F1 remains in the supplied draft; the supported repair above has not been applied because this task authorises only the report.

This is the commissioned author’s voice. It expresses the association’s position, not an invented assertion about the user or a counted membership. |
| “us” in the heading and explanatory argument | First person, plural, no natural gender. Rhetorical inclusion of author and readers in evaluating evidence. No shared personal experience, actual knowledge, action or measured reader belief is claimed. The source’s intended readers are “invånarna i fiktiva Lervik”. |
| “Those” | Plural demonstrative referring to the 96 and 24 bookings. Source explicitly counts “bokningar, inte unika personer”. It asserts no number of distinct humans. |
| “they” in “why they choose” | Third person, plural, gender-neutral, referring to users asked during the proposed trial. Source has plural “användarna … varför de väljer telefon eller webb”. Both referent and grammatical number are supplied; no natural gender is inferred. |
| “It” in “It gives us a reason” | Third person, singular, nonhuman antecedent: telephone booking/using that route. Matches “Det är ett skäl …”. No natural-gender assertion. |
| “its” in “its value”, “it” in “the work it requires” | Third person, singular, nonhuman booking-route referent. Source discusses the retained telephone/web channels and weighing labour cost against user value. No human or gender assertion. |

Source “kommunstyrelsen … Därefter kan den avgöra …” supplies that referent and institutional singular. Swedish grammatical common gender in “den” does not become a human natural-gender claim. |

Other demonstratives (“that route”, “That concern”, “that trial”, “After that”) refer respectively to telephone booking, the stated double-administration objection, the proposed six-month trial, and the preceding proposed sequence. Their referents are supplied; they carry no natural gender. “Both” and “each” refer to the two supplied channels, telephone and web. The draft introduces no he/she pronoun or other natural-gender assertion about any person. Sanna’s name alone has not been treated as gender evidence.

## Findings

### F1 — Source-support: measurement exclusion broadened across languages

Draft: “The report measures neither age nor functional ability nor **familiarity with digital technology**.”

Source: “Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller **digital vana**.”

The third term changes what is excluded from measurement. “Digital vana” concerns being accustomed to using digital technology. “Familiarity with digital technology” can also mean knowing about it without practical accustomedness. The source therefore does not establish every measurement absence the English sentence can assert.

**Source-true/draft-false case:** the pilot report could include a test of users’ knowledge of digital tools learned from printed manuals, while measuring neither their practical accustomedness to using those tools, age, nor functional ability. It could still count bookings rather than unique people. Such a report would satisfy the supplied exclusions but would measure a form of familiarity. No supplied statement excludes that case; the source does not describe every other possible measure in the report.

**Reverse-direction test:** a report measuring users’ habitual digital use while omitting a separate knowledge assessment could distinguish practical accustomedness from knowledge-based familiarity. As a case for this actual report, however, it is excluded by the explicit statement “Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana.” The surviving first-direction case is sufficient to establish the draft’s broader absence claim.

**Smallest supported repair:** “The report measures neither age nor functional ability nor **how accustomed users are to using digital technology**.” This retains the measured attributes, certainty and report attribution while narrowing the third term to the supplied one.

No other source-support defect was found. The administration’s explanation remains attributed in its complete sentence; the report’s counts and exclusions remain qualified; the organisation’s absence of a claim is not turned into knowledge of non-occurrence; and the author’s advocacy is not treated as an empirical finding.

## Quotation and translation findings

There is no direct speech quotation in the draft. The supplied quotation is paraphrased with its evidential point and author attribution preserved. There is no translated-quotation finding. F1 concerns the source support of non-quoted prose, rather than quotation voice or idiom. General editorial and mechanical review were not undertaken.

Completion status: complete. Unresolved findings: F1 remains in the supplied draft; the supported repair above has not been applied because this task authorises only the report.

Every other genre leaves this step empty. Done when counted requirements and heading pairs have been considered, repairs are complete, and any remaining defects are carried forward.\\n8. Done when the draft has been delivered, or the run has stopped with its prose preserved and accounted for.\\n\"}}}"}, {"type": "input_text", "text": "{\"read\":1,\"result\":{\"status\":\"fulfilled\",\"value\":{\"chunk_id\":\"275b7c\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000001667,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":673,\"output\":\"# Opinion brief and source\\n\\nSYNTHETIC EVALUATION MATERIAL. Fictional policy debate, not legal advice or a real municipal process. Source language: Swedish.\\n\\n## Brief\\n\\nSkriv en debattartikel för invånarna i fiktiva Lervik. Avsändare är Sanna Ek, talesperson för föreningen Öppna beslut. Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb. En tydlig, sakligt skarp text på ungefär 400 ord. Markdown med orienterande rubriker. Ingen kommersiell CTA.\\n\\n## Underlag\\n\\nKommunens pilotrapport Bokning av föreningslokaler, 8 april 2026, gäller ett åtta veckor långt försök i två lokaler. Under försöket gjordes 96 bokningar via webb och 24 via telefon. Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer. Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana. Telefonbokningarna kan därför inte användas för att säga hur stor andel av invånarna som inte kan boka digitalt.\\n\\nTjänsteutlåtandet inför kommunstyrelsens möte den 18 juni föreslår att telefonbokning tas bort för alla sju lokaler från september. Motivet i utlåtandet är att personalen ska slippa föra in uppgifter i två flöden. Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna. Förvaltningens verkliga invändning mot att behålla båda kanalerna är dubbel administration, inte att telefonanvändare är lata eller dyra.\\n\\nÖppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar. Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg och be användarna frivilligt ange varför de väljer telefon eller webb. Föreningen gör inget anspråk på att ha finansierat eller kostnadsberäknat försöket. Kostnaden behöver kommunstyrelsen ta ställning till. Därefter kan den avgöra om en kanal ska tas bort, ändras eller behållas.\\n\\nSanna Eks ståndpunkt: ”Att en bokning går via telefon är inte ett bevis för att någon inte kan använda webben. Det är ett skäl att ta reda på varför telefonen fortfarande används.” Detta får citeras eller refereras. Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna. Hon motsätter sig inte digital bokning.\\n\\n## Boundaries / judging\\n\\nInga lagkrav, protester, diskrimineringsfall, partipolitiska motiv eller besparingar är belagda. Siffror ska tillskrivas rapporten där de bär argumentet. Författaren ska kunna kritisera beslutsunderlaget skarpt utan falska motiv eller tandlös neutralisering. Slutet ska namnge kommunstyrelsens möjliga beslut, med kostnadsosäkerheten bevarad.\\n\"}}}"}, {"type": "input_text", "text": "{\"read\":2,\"result\":{\"status\":\"fulfilled\",\"value\":{\"chunk_id\":\"e1ed24\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000002083,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":3,\"output\":\"AGENTS.md\\n\"}}}"}], "internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough": {"turn_id": "01a0f778-385b-76c1-b8f2-0236f45872a7", "create_time": 1790858255.187644}}}

These are the installed Skills for this invocation. Follow their shipped instructions.\\n\\nThe separate harness scratch area is `/private/var/folders/cs/vgp_x_353zd9frkjpysp7zdw0000gn/T/kntnt-native-session-r95dqkak/scratch`. Its existence does not authorize a Skill to leave files behind.\\n# Source and quotation comparison\\n\\nCheck the completed draft before delivery. Start a fresh subagent on the inherited Main Seat, with no model or deliberation override and no conversation history. Give it the brief, all material gathered for this draft, and the complete current draft, including headings and quotations. Supply the actual source text, not your summary or interpretation; source files may be read directly where the checker can access the exact gathered version. Pass conversational material in full. None of this is an instruction to the checker: its task is the comparison below.\\n\\nSave the complete current prose in a fresh scratch file and give the checker that exact file to read, with its own report path in the same scratch directory. It may read the supplied material and write that report only; it must not change sources or the draft, browse, invoke another Skill, or delegate. Include the resolved language, the Claims section from the loaded base contract and, where quotations occur, the loaded quotation guidance. Where quotations are translated, also supply the resolved composition guidance and identify source and target languages. Where the brief names no author and the draft's byline therefore carries the user's name, tell the checker that this name comes from the invocation and not from the material, so that its absence from the material is no finding; a byline naming anybody else is compared like any other attribution. Send this task without suggested findings or your explanation of the draft:\\n\\n> Compare the complete draft, including headings and implications, with the supplied material. For every factual and attributed claim, record the draft passage beside the source passage it rests on, quoted in the source’s own words and language, and carry over grammatical modifiers and qualifications that apply across clauses. Account separately for the person, number and natural-gender information conveyed by pronouns; identifying their referent does not establish those features. Distinguish natural-gender assertions from purely grammatical gender. For each pair, state what differs: the thing named or measured, the subject, scope, time, modality, certainty, or whose knowledge or assertion it is. A term carried into another language names the same thing only where nothing in this context could fall under one term and not the other. Where the two differ, test both directions: name a concrete case, compatible with the supplied material, in which one statement holds and the other fails. The case need only be compatible with the supplied material; the material need not describe it or make it likely. Set a case aside only by quoting a supplied statement that excludes it. If a case stands, that is a finding, however natural or cautious the draft’s wording; if you can name none, it is not. A claim with no source passage is a finding unless it is the author’s own argument, reflection or advocacy resting on supported material. Read such a passage as an ordinary reader would in its context, and test the facts, events, experiences and views it presents as given, not the reasoning or transition itself. Read every statement for what it does in its context and preserve who stands behind it: a valuation, a rhetorical generalisation or a position the brief gives as the commissioning party’s own is supported as that party’s standpoint wherever the brief carries it, and needs no external evidence to be expressed as theirs. That the brief establishes who asserts something establishes nothing about whether it is so. A figure, an event, a technical effect or a claim about an actual population stays a factual claim whoever supplied it, and calling one an opinion supports nothing. Where the line between a standpoint and a factual assertion is genuinely unsettled, report that passage as the brief’s own unevidenced statement or as an editorial question rather than as a defect with a repair. A finding is a defect in the draft rather than a preference about it; do not report what is defensible as written. For each finding, show the support that is missing or changed and propose the smallest supported repair. Preserve supported claims, warranted certainty, voice and editorial choices. Judge source support. A meaning changed in crossing languages outside quoted speech is a source-support finding. For translated quotations, separately check that the same meaning and distinctive voice are expressed in idiomatic target-language speech. Identify a concrete obstruction to that reading, not merely a preferred synonym; clarify an implicit referent only where the supplied context settles it. General editorial and mechanical review are outside this comparison. Distinguish source-support findings from translation findings. Write the complete claim accounting and findings to the report path, ending with completion status and any unresolved findings. Reply in at most 150 words with completion status and the report path. Where you cannot write that file, say in your reply that the file could not be written and why, and give the complete report there instead, ending it the same way; the 150-word limit does not apply to that reply.\\n\\nUse the Harness's native completion/wait mechanism to await the checker's completion and returned reply. Never start a shell or background task that polls for the report path: the file may be absent until you save the reply yourself. Where the Harness has no usable completion mechanism, name that comparison obstacle and follow the incomplete-comparison procedure below.\\n\\nAfter checker completion, consume the report by whichever route the Harness allows, without waiting for the other route. Where the checker could not write its file, its reply carries the report instead: save that text to the report path yourself and read it from there. What settles a comparison is the report and not its route, and what settles the report is its last lines: the task has every report end with its completion status and any unresolved findings, so a report carrying that ending is complete by either route and one lacking it is partial by either route, however far its accounting appears to reach. A comparison cut off before it finished leaves accounting that reads as whole, and only the stated completion status tells the two apart. A checker that completes without a complete report leaves an incomplete comparison; a missing or partial report, interruption, timeout or transport failure follows that same procedure, never a further wait for a file. None makes unchecked prose checked.\\n\\nRead the complete report and validate its claim accounting as well as its findings, after either comparison. Verify that the complete draft is covered and that every pairing sets the draft passage, in its actual contextual meaning and with its qualifications, beside the source passage it rests on, even when the report lists no findings. A faulty pairing does not by itself make the draft faulty. First establish what the draft actually asserts in its full context, then whether the supplied material supports that proposition with its subject, scope and qualifications intact. A merely conceivable stronger reading does not establish a defect. Distinguish a report that has not established its allegation from genuine material ambiguity or unresolved support in the draft. Reject a finding only with exact draft and source evidence showing support or a concrete mismatch in the report’s reading. For a translation finding, use the resolved composition and quotation guidance to establish the target-language reading. Confidence, preferred wording or qualifications present only in the source settle neither kind of finding. Never go looking for outside legitimation of a standpoint already identified as somebody’s own: research answers a relevant, answerable and bounded question of evidence, and scattered examples of people saying a thing establish no “everybody”. Record detected accounting mismatches and each finding’s disposition and evidence beside the report.\\n\\nMake only supported repairs, preserving the rest of the draft. If the first comparison leads to any change or disputed part of the report, give a fresh checker the complete current draft and original material for one further comparison under the same task. Supply neither the earlier findings nor your defence. There are at most two comparisons, and validate a second report by the same standard. The final comparison is the last one this run completed, whether that is the second or the first: a comparison that did not complete is no comparison at all, so where a further one fails and the prose is exactly what the earlier complete comparison read, that earlier one is final, while prose repaired since has been read by no complete comparison. After it, change no prose at all, not even into a repair the checker itself proposed: a repair no comparison has read is unchecked prose however safe it looks, and what is delivered is the prose the final comparison read.\\n\\nWhere a complete comparison of the current prose leaves no accepted defect and no genuinely unresolved material claim, proceed. Where the final comparison leaves either, deliver the prose exactly as that comparison read it and report every remaining finding beside the draft: the draft passage, the concrete problem, what the supplied material carries instead, and the smallest repair the checker proposed where it proposed one. A remaining finding is what the comparison alleges and the editor settles, so report it as that and never as an established fact about the text. The account says the draft is delivered with known defects before it says anything else about the draft, and it asserts no fidelity for the passages it names. A rejected finding is the writer’s evidenced decision, not the checker’s approval.\\n\\nReport the findings beside the text rather than in it: in the delivery account, and not inside the draft or the file at the Output Target, because what is written there is prose a later Skill reads as the text. Where the user asks for the findings marked in the document itself, do that instead: the prose stays exactly as the final comparison read it, and each marking is plainly editorial and separable from it, so nothing carries on as publishable prose. Where that document goes to a path, it also carries the same accounting once, as one clearly editorial block, so what the editor has to act on does not exist only in this reply.\\n\\nStopping is for prose no comparison has read. A comparison that could not finish, material that could not be supplied in full, and a missing or partial report are each no completed comparison, whichever route it arrived by. Where that leaves the current prose with no complete comparison of its own, nothing is delivered: report the concrete obstacle and say what was and was not compared. Where an earlier comparison completed and the prose is exactly what it read, because a disputed report alone sent an unchanged draft on, that comparison is the final one: deliver the draft as it read the prose, report every remaining finding beside it, and say in the account which comparison did not finish and what it therefore left uncompared. Nothing unread is ever delivered, and a comparison that failed over its own route or its own material is no reason to withhold prose already compared in full.\\n\\nA stop never destroys the draft. Preserve the current prose where the user can still reach it once this session is over. Where the Output Target is a path, write the prose to a distinct path beside that target whose name cannot be mistaken for the requested artifact, leave the Output Target itself unwritten, and report that path with the comparison status; where the Output Target is the response, carry the complete current prose in the reply, marked as not source-checked, and carried the way `$LIBRARY/references/delivery.md` says a response carries a text. Say which comparisons ran and what remains, so the work can be resumed rather than written again, and never present preserved prose as checked or delivered.\\n\\nAfter the final comparison, preserve the compared prose exactly; only the resolved Handoff Metadata and delivery wrapper remain to add. Read the report before removing its scratch directory, and carry whatever a remaining finding needs in order to be understood and acted on into the delivery account first. Only genuine scratch is removed on any exit: the draft and that accounting are not scratch. The report itself is internal working material, not an extra user deliverable.\\n\\nKeep every necessary wait helper bounded and owned by this run, and retain the identities of the checker and any helpers you start. On completion, failure, timeout, cancellation or interruption, stop outstanding run-owned waiters and delegated work before removing scratch. Verify their terminal state through the Harness or process/task state so they cannot write into scratch after cleanup. Do not terminate unrelated processes or another run's checker. If stopping or verification fails, preserve the draft and needed accounting, retain scratch still reachable by active work, and report cleanup failure with the outstanding identities and obstacle; never claim the run left nothing. Report cleanup failure alongside the comparison and delivery status, without turning unchecked prose into checked prose.\\n# Quoting speech\\n\\nRead this before putting quotation marks around anything somebody said. It applies to interview transcripts, recorded remarks, meeting notes, and any other material where the words are a person's rather than a document's.\\n\\nSpeech is not written prose, and a transcript quoted raw reads as carelessness towards the speaker rather than fidelity to them. So a spoken quotation may be repaired — cautiously, within a boundary that is easier to state than to feel your way to.\\n\\nWhen translating permitted quotations, preserve meaning, stance, certainty and distinctive voice in idiomatic target-language speech, not source-language syntax. The restrictions below protect that substance; they do not require word-for-word translation. Make an implicit referent explicit only when the supplied context settles it. Where the meaning remains ambiguous, paraphrase what is established rather than inventing a fluent interpretation.\\n\\n## What may be repaired\\n\\nThe syntax of speech: a sentence abandoned halfway and restarted, a subject left behind by its verb, a clause that never closes. Fillers — *you know*, *sort of*, *liksom*, *alltså* — where they carry nothing. Repetition that is the speaker searching for a word rather than insisting on one. False starts, and the small disfluencies a person does not hear themselves make.\\n\\nThat is the whole list. Everything on it is noise the speaker did not intend to produce, and removing it leaves what they meant to say.\\n\\n## What may not\\n\\n**Meaning.** Not narrowed, not widened, not sharpened. A speaker who said *most of them* has not said *all of them*, and one who said *we looked at it* has not said *we rejected it*.\\n\\n**Stance.** Approval stays approval, reluctance stays reluctance, and a speaker who was careful not to blame anybody is not made to blame somebody by a tidier sentence.\\n\\n**Certainty.** *I think*, *probably*, *as far as I know*, and *we are not sure yet* are the claim, not padding on it. Removing a hedge manufactures a confidence the speaker did not have; adding one takes away a confidence they did.\\n\\n**Distinctive wording.** Where the speaker reached for an unusual word, an image, a piece of slang, or a phrasing that is theirs, it stays. Smoothing it into the register of the surrounding article is the most tempting of these failures and the one a reader can least detect.\\n\\n**A self-correction.** Where a speaker gives a number or a fact and then corrects it, the correction is what they said. Quoting the first version is a misquotation; quoting a version that never existed — the correction with the correcting away — is worse.\\n\\n**Anything additive.** Add no new meaning: no fact, stance, certainty, name, figure or connection that the speaker did not supply. A permitted repair or translation may change words only to render that same meaning.\\n\\n## When to paraphrase instead\\n\\nWhere fidelity is uncertain, paraphrase. A paraphrase that attributes carefully — *she said the change had taken about a year, and that she was not sure it would hold* — is accurate, useful, and honest about what the material supports. A fluent direct quotation assembled out of a fragmentary answer is none of those things, however good it sounds.\\n\\nThe cases that should turn into paraphrase: material that is a summary of what somebody said rather than their words; a transcript too broken to repair inside the boundary above; several remarks that would have to be welded together to make one sentence; and any moment where you find yourself choosing between two readings of what the speaker meant.\\n\\nSignal what the material actually is. Where the source is notes rather than a recording, the text says what the person said and does not pretend to their exact wording.\\n\\n## What stays outside this\\n\\nWhether the speaker approves the quotation. Quote approval is a human arrangement between the writer and the person quoted, and nothing here verifies it or stands in for it. A draft may say a quotation is unapproved; it may not treat the absence of approval as a problem it has solved.\\n\"}}}\n{\"read\":1,\"result\":{\"status\":\"fulfilled\",\"value\":{\"chunk_id\":\"57a38f\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000001959,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":5189,\"output\":\"# The base contract\\n\\nWrite with the clarity and factual precision of a well-edited newspaper or quality magazine.
