Criticise ideas, decisions and actions precisely; do not invent an opponent's motives. Preserve warranted sharpness as well as material qualifications, without compulsory softening or inflated rhetoric.\\n\\nThe call to action is the specific change or stance the argument supports, and the ending makes clear who can act. Reconsidering or declining a proposal can be the action; a generic exhortation alone is not one.\\n\\n## The technique this genre is ordinarily written with\\n\\nNone.\\n# Delivery\\n\\nEvery Skill that produces a Text Artifact delivers it the same way. This document is the complete contract: where a result goes, how a response carries the text, what the response carries when it went somewhere else, when a source file may be replaced instead, what happens when nothing changed, what language a run's own words about the text are written in and whether what those words say is true of the text they name, and what is refused before anything is written. It is a reference several Skills read rather than a runtime of its own. Each Skill exposes these rules through its own Formal Invocation and owns the names it gives them, so nothing here fixes how an option is spelled; what is fixed is the behaviour behind it.\\n\\n## The response is the default\\n\\nThe default Output Target is the agent response. A run that keeps the default delivers the complete Text Artifact in the response and changes nothing on the filesystem: it creates no file, touches no file, and makes no directory. That includes scratch files and working copies of the artifact, wherever the Harness says temporary files belong.\\n\\nA Harness convention identifies where an authorized temporary file belongs; it does not authorize a write. If a run genuinely needs a scratch file while composing, it removes that file before delivery and restores the filesystem to the state in which it found it. Before reporting delivery, the run checks the filesystem locations it used, including the Harness scratch area, and never reports that nothing was written while a copy of the artifact remains on disk.\\n\\nAn Output Target is independent of where the source material came from. Supplying a local file as source material selects no destination, and a run that reads a file still delivers to the response until the caller names somewhere else. Persisting a result is the caller's explicit choice, and making that choice is the whole of the authorization for it.\\n\\n## How a response carries the text\\n\\nA response carries a Text Artifact inside a fenced code block rather than as prose of the reply's own. A text and the words about it are two different things, and a reply that runs them together hands the reader a text nothing marks the edges of. Markdown is where that costs most: an artifact opening on a frontmatter delimiter, set directly beneath a paragraph the run wrote, turns that paragraph into a heading unless a blank line goes in between them — and a blank line there is what ends the frontmatter block the text was settled with. A fence removes the question rather than leaving each run to answer it, and the answers a run improvises are what made one saved reply in sixteen deliver a horizontal rule and a paragraph of configuration in place of a frontmatter block.\\n\\nThe complete Text Artifact sits inside exactly one fenced code block, byte for byte as the run settled it: frontmatter, blank lines and trailing structure included. Nothing is added, dropped or re-wrapped between the fence lines, and one block rather than several, a text split across two fences being a text the reader has to reassemble.\\n\\nThe fence is longer than the longest run of backticks inside the artifact, so a text carrying fenced code of its own cannot close it early. The info string names the text's format: `markdown` for a Markdown text, `html` for HTML, `text` for plain text.\\n\\nEverything the run says *about* the text sits outside the fence — the resolved configuration, the findings and which of them are unresolved, the claim account, where a technique came from, what the run could not do. Nothing of the run's own is inside it. What the run settled as part of the document does belong inside, the Handoff Metadata and any marking the user asked to have placed in the document itself among it, because that is the text as delivered rather than a remark about it.\\n\\nThe rule reaches every complete text a reply carries, whether this contract calls that text delivered or not. A response-targeted run that changed nothing and has findings left fences the artifact like any other; a run that stopped before it could deliver and carries its preserved prose in the reply fences that prose, and says in its own words outside the fence what the prose has not been through. The short no-change status carries no text at all and so carries no fence. An explicit destination and In-place Editing receive the text itself and never a fence: a fence is how a reply holds a text apart from its own words, and a file has no words of its own to be held apart from.\\n\\n## An explicit destination\\n\\nAn explicit destination is one filesystem path, and what exists at that path decides what happens to it.\\n\\n**A path that does not exist** creates exactly that one file and writes the complete Text Artifact into it. Exactly one file: nothing else is created beside it, and no directory is made to hold it.\\n\\n**A path naming an existing file** overwrites that exact file with the complete Text Artifact. No confirmation and no separate force option is required, and none is offered. Naming the path is the authorization, and demanding a second gesture on top of it would teach that naming a destination means something less than it says.\\n\\n**A path naming an existing directory** delivers into that directory under a filename the run derives, by the rules below. Choosing a directory selects a place, not a file, so it never overwrites a file already in it.\\n\\n## Deriving a filename for a directory\\n\\nA directory destination needs a name, and the name comes from what the run already knows. Take the first of these the run has: the basename of the local file that supplied the Text Artifact, the title of a referenced URL, then the Skill's own working title for the text it created. Reduce it to a name the filesystem accepts, and keep a suitable text extension — the source file's own where it had one, and otherwise the one matching the text's format.\\n\\nThat gives a stem and an extension, and the stem stays as it is for every delivery into that directory. Where nothing exists at the derived name, that name is the file written. Where something exists there, the existing file is never overwritten: keep the original stem and take the first free numbered candidate in ascending order, beginning at `-2`. So `my-file.md`, `my-file-2.md`, `my-file-3.md` are one sequence, growing by one name at a time.\\n\\nA numbered candidate is never adopted as the new stem. A third delivery into a directory already holding the first two is `my-file-3.md` and never `my-file-2-2.md`, whose stem would fork the sequence in two and leave the directory recording how many times the same text arrived rather than which arrival it was. Ascending order with the first free name also means a number freed by a deleted file is used again before a higher one is taken.\\n\\n## What the response carries when a destination was named\\n\\nA run that delivers to an explicit destination does not repeat the Text Artifact in the response. The response says where the text went — the derived filename included, where the destination was a directory — and carries what the file cannot: the findings the run reports beside the text, and which of them are unresolved. Naming a destination is saying where the text goes, and printing it again spends output on what the caller is already holding, doubly so when the file just written holds it too.\\n\\nThat holds whether or not the pass changed anything. A run that writes a file byte-identical to the source it read reports the destination and says the text needed no work, and does not become the one run that echoes a text the caller had before it started. The response is the default target precisely because a run that names no destination has nowhere else to put the artifact; naming one moves the text out of the response rather than duplicating it there.\\n\\n## In-place Editing\\n\\nIn-place Editing replaces the single local file that supplied the Text Artifact with the result, instead of delivering it anywhere else. It is available only to a Skill whose work is to return a changed version of a text the caller already has.\\n\\nA Skill that creates a new Text Artifact never edits its own source material in place. The brief, the interview, the notes, and the article it draws on are where the text came from and not where it goes; a Skill that could overwrite them would make supplying a file a risk rather than a convenience, and would lose the material the result is answerable to.\\n\\nIn-place Editing requires exactly one writable local source file, and it is mutually exclusive with a separate output option. A run replaces its source or delivers elsewhere, never both, and an invocation asking for both has named two destinations for one text.\\n\\nIt is refused wherever there is no such file to replace. Text supplied inline in the invocation or taken from the conversation is not a file. A URL is not a local file, and fetching one grants no right to write anything back. An uploaded source and a read-only file cannot be replaced. More than one Text Artifact leaves the single file the contract is written around undefined.\\n\\nAn explicit output path equal to the input path is refused in favour of In-place Editing, and the refusal says which gesture to use. Replacing a source then has one recognizable authorization: a reader of an invocation sees that a source is about to be replaced from the request itself, without comparing two paths to discover it.\\n\\n## When nothing changed\\n\\nA run may finish with nothing to change, and what it does then follows its destination.\\n\\nA response-targeted run that changed nothing returns a short no-change status in place of the text when no unresolved findings remain. When findings remain, deliver the complete Text Artifact in the response and report the unresolved findings separately.\\n\\nAn in-place run that changed nothing leaves the source file untouched and returns the short status with any unresolved findings. Rewriting a file with its own contents would make its timestamp claim that something happened.\\n\\nThat status is written in the language of the Text Artifact rather than the language of the invocation, so a Swedish text that needed no work is reported on in Swedish.\\n\\nAn explicitly selected different file or directory still receives the complete Text Artifact when nothing changed. Creating that artifact is what was asked for, and a destination left empty because the text needed no work is a request refused without saying so. A directory destination derives its filename and resolves collisions exactly as it would for a changed text, and the response reports the destination without repeating the artifact beside it, exactly as for a run that changed something.\\n\\n## The language of a report about the text\\n\\nA run that reports on the text beside the text itself — the findings it carries forward, and which of them are unresolved — writes that report in the language of the Text Artifact rather than the language of the invocation, exactly as the no-change status above is written. So a Swedish artifact reviewed from an English-language invocation comes back with Swedish findings, whether the run corrected anything or was asked to correct nothing at all, and one text never produces a status in its own language beside a finding list in another.\\n\\nWho reads it is what decides this. A report about a text is read by the reader of that text, who has to finish whatever the run left undone, and a report in whichever language the invocation happened to be written in reaches that reader in a language nothing about the text promised. The invocation's language is how the run was asked for; the artifact's language is what the run is about.\\n\\n## The truth of a report about the text\\n\\nEverything a run says about a text is true of the text it names. That holds for every statement the reply makes about a text, and not only for its findings and its account of the claims: a count, a length, a grammatical label, where a passage stands, and what a round did to a passage — moved it, removed it, replaced it, set a heading over it — each tells the reader something about the text they could check, and each is written so that the check holds.\\n\\nA statement about the delivered text is checked against the delivered text after the last change to it, the closing mechanical pass included. Most of what a run knows about a text it learned while reviewing the text as it arrived, and the rounds changed that text afterwards, so a length measured before a round or a heading remembered from one describes a text the reader is no longer holding. Where a run measures a text with the Collection Library's measuring script, `scripts/article_anatomy.py`, a count or length the reply states is taken from that script's measurement of the text the statement is about, never from the run's own reading of it and never from a measurement of another state of the text. A statement about the text as it arrived says so, so that nobody reads it against the text delivered. A statement that cannot be made true of the text it names is left out.\\n\\nThe check is made on the reply as drafted, one statement at a time, once the text it describes is final, and each statement is read against the passage it names rather than against what the run remembers of it; a finding written during the review is checked in the same way against the text it was written about. A statement that something holds everywhere or nowhere — that a passage is unchanged, word for word what it was or untouched, that the rest of the text was left alone, that a name appears nowhere else, that a heading says nothing about its section — is checked against every part of the text it covers, because a single exception makes it false, and where there is one the statement names it or is narrowed to what holds. A statement of where wording came from, or of where a passage now stands, is checked by finding that passage in the text.\\n\\nWho reads it decides this too. The reader of a report finds their way in the text by it, and a statement they check and find false costs them the trust they would have given every other statement in the same reply.\\n\\n## Refusals\\n\\nEvery contradictory, meaningless, or unusable output request is refused before the first side effect. The cases this contract names are more than one Text Artifact where the Skill processes one, an output option together with In-place Editing, an output path equal to the input path, In-place Editing for inline text or a URL or an uploaded or read-only source, and a destination the run cannot write — a path whose parent directory does not exist among them.\\n\\nEvery such check that can be made by reading is made before anything is written, so a refusal leaves no partial effect behind: no file created, none truncated, none renamed, and no directory made. Where a conflict can only be discovered after a legitimate effect, the run stops there and reports the exact partial outcome rather than continuing.\\n\\nA refusal follows the Collection's ordinary diagnostic and help conventions. It names what was wrong, prints the synopsis of the most specific recognized command page verbatim, and points at that command's own help form. An output option is refused rather than ignored where it has no work to do, a flag accepted and ignored teaching that flags sometimes do prose is understated. It states a strong claim plainly and lets the claim carry the emphasis, where American register would reach for an intensifier. Irony and litotes are available and are not read as evasion.\\\\n\\\\nCollective nouns take plural agreement when the members are acting and singular when the body is acting as one: *the team are arguing about it*, *the committee has published its report*. Both are correct; what matters is that the choice is consistent within a passage.\\\\n\\\\nThe present perfect covers a recent past that still bears on now: *I've just seen the figures*, *have you eaten?* The simple past in those positions reads as American.\\\\n\\\\n*Have got* is ordinary British usage for possession and obligation — *I've got the file*, *you've got to see this* — and is not an informality to be edited out of general prose.\\\\n\\\\nPrepositions differ in a small, fixed set of places: *at the weekend*, *in hospital*, *different from* or *different to*, *write to somebody*, *meet somebody* rather than *meet with*. None of these is a preference; each is the British form.\\\\n\\\\nSpelling is *-ise* or *-ize* by house style — both are correct British English, *-ise* being the majority convention and *-ize* the Oxford one — but a document uses one throughout. Genre, audience, and purpose override that register: a letter, a speech and an advertisement keep their own character. The selected genre governs craft and form; the resolved language governs idiom, syntax and typography. Examples here illustrate meanings, not English phrases to translate.\\n\\n## Reader and progression\\n\\nGive the intended reader what they need to understand, judge or do the thing the text is for. Keep a clear focus, introduce unfamiliar concepts before the reasoning needs them, and order material by that reader's needs rather than the writer's discovery process. Relevant technical detail belongs; unexplained jargon and unrelated background do not.\\n\\nMake the relations between passages real. A transition may explain a connection, but cannot invent one. Keep paragraphs coherent and give supporting material a clear place beside what it supports. The body and its sections remain intelligible without treating a title, standfirst or subheading as their opening sentence.\\n\\nOpen on something specific to the subject. End with what the job calls for: an earned understanding, conclusion, recommendation or next step. Remove introductions and conclusions that add only an announcement or a generic flourish.\\n\\n## Expression\\n\\nUse concrete words, useful verbs and a natural, varied cadence. Keep terminology and reference clear; introduce an unfamiliar abbreviation before using it alone. Choose ordinary words where they suffice and established specialist terms where they give this reader precision.\\n\\nLet tone and rhetorical devices serve the genre and the meaning. A useful analogy, question, image, fragment or humorous turn can clarify or carry a voice; decoration and manufactured drama cannot. An imagined comparison is recognisable as a comparison, never presented as an event or someone's experience. Criticism addresses the supported issue without inventing motives or patronising the reader.\\n\\nEach passage earns its place. Repetition may establish an independent entry point, explain a hard idea or give a deliberate rhetorical return; merely saying the same thing again does none of these. Keep formatting useful: headings for orientation, lists for coordinate items, tables for comparisons, emphasis for what merits it. Hold voice, terminology and presentation consistent unless a visible purpose calls for a change.\\n\\n## Claims\\n\\nEvery claim is supported by the text or its material, with exact attribution and proportionate strength. Preserve the state of knowledge: what is unknown or unclaimed is not thereby known not to have happened. Keep uncertainty where it exists and certainty where it is warranted. A fact, quotation, source, scene, personal experience or opinion attributed to somebody is never invented to complete a form. Name the actual source of an attributed claim rather than invoking unnamed studies or experts.\\n\\nCircumstantial detail is a claim too: duration, manner, motive, absence and background all need support. So does an evaluative characterisation: calling a count high, low or modest needs a comparison, target, capacity or speaker assessment. Otherwise give the count with its exclusions, without that assessment.\\n\\nA sequence or correlation establishes no cause. A causal verb such as *shortened* needs causal support; a causal hedge such as *suggests that it shortened* still adds that attribution. This boundary holds in the title, summary, headings and body alike. Preserve chronology, scope and qualifications wherever a claim appears.\\n\\nA stated length constrains the use of material and is never a licence to add to it. When the material is insufficient, deliver the length the material supports and name what further material would close the gap. A supplied publication limit is binding; meeting it cannot justify invention or loss of a qualification that changes a claim.\\n# Opinion\\n\\nA journalistic debate article that argues a clear position and asks for a change or a considered stance. The author takes responsibility for the argument rather than observing it from outside.\\n\\nApply the shared [web craft brief](../web-craft.md).\\n\\nFollow the [article anatomy](../article-anatomy.md).\\n\\nWrite the headline and the subheadings as [Headlines](../headlines.md) says.\\n\\n## What this genre asks for\\n\\nState the thesis early and identify its holder when an author is supplied. Let the headline and useful subheadings carry the issue and argument for a scanning reader.\\n\\nBuild a case the reader can follow, distinguishing evidence, judgement and inference. Attribute load-bearing factual claims where they are used, and meet relevant real objections fairly. Neither an argument quota nor an invented opponent makes the case stronger.\\n\\nKeep a persuasive, accountable voice without strengthening or weakening the author's supplied positions: absence of opposition is not endorsement. Criticise ideas, decisions and actions precisely; do not invent an opponent's motives. Preserve warranted sharpness as well as material qualifications, without compulsory softening or inflated rhetoric.\\n\\nThe call to action is the specific change or stance the argument supports, and the ending makes clear who can act. Reconsidering or declining a proposal can be the action; a generic exhortation alone is not one.\\n\\n## The technique this genre is ordinarily written with\\n\\nNone.\\n\"}}}"}, {"type": "input_text", "text": "{\"read\":1,\"result\":{\"status\":\"fulfilled\",\"value\":{\"chunk_id\":\"0f17bd\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000002458,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":2879,\"output\":\"# Craft for five web genres\\n\\nFor article, casestudy, column and opinion, work as a skilled Swedish journalist at the professional level of SvD, DN, DI, Fokus, Ny Teknik, Filter or Forskning & Framsteg. For webcopy, work as an experienced Swedish copywriter and UX writer trusted by leading agencies and brands. These are standards of craft, not voices to imitate.\\n\\nIn every target language, keep that Swedish editorial or communicative tradition in the angle, disposition, storytelling, address and tone. Think and write wholly in the target language: its Language Resource governs vocabulary, idiom, syntax, punctuation and typography. A native professional should hear a colleague, not a translation. Language choice changes the language surface, not the chosen craft.\\n\\n## Web reading\\n\\nGive the reader a clear angle or task and useful ways into the text. Let facts and reasoning carry explanation; choose any narrative devices for what they contribute. Headings orient according to the genre, paragraphs hold connected thoughts, and varied rhythm supports both scanning and sustained reading.\\n\\nFor article, casestudy, column and opinion, the [article anatomy](article-anatomy.md) fixes the parts, their order and their dimensions.\\n# Article anatomy\\n\\nThe fixed skeleton of a text in the genres `article`, `casestudy`, `column` and `opinion`; *article* below means a text in any of them. The anatomy fixes which parts the text has, their order and their dimensions. Angle, argument, voice and language are outside it.\\n\\nA statement below has one of three strengths:\\n\\n- A plain statement is a requirement: it holds in every conforming text.\\n- *Should* marks a norm: follow it as a requirement, and depart only when following it is impossible or the departure is clearly better for the reader.\\n- *Most* states what is typical across the whole text; each single paragraph or section follows its own content.\\n\\nA text conforms when every part is present in the order shown and all three hold. Limits are exact; verify each one by counting.\\n\\nA review of a finished text reports a part the text lacks as missing rather than writing it, and repairs only the parts the text has.\\n\\n## Order\\n\\n```text\\nHeadline\\nStandfirst\\nByline\\nLead\\nSubheading        ┐ a section; one or more\\nParagraphs        ┘\\nLast subheading   ┐ the ending\\nParagraphs        ┘\\n```\\n\\nEach part appears once, except the section, which repeats. The first subheading follows the lead directly. The body is everything from the lead through the ending.\\n\\nThe headline is the text's level-1 heading (HTML `h1`, Markdown `#`), and every subheading is a level-2 heading (HTML `h2`, Markdown `##`). The text should use these two levels only. Where an academic text would nest a level-3 heading, a further level-2 subheading usually serves; a level-3 heading is for the rare text whose structure truly demands one.\\n\\n## Headline\\n\\n- 20–70 characters, spaces included.\\n- It should be three to eight words and at most 60 characters.\\n- It is written as [Headlines](headlines.md) says.\\n\\n## Standfirst\\n\\nThe standfirst is a summarising paragraph that introduces the article and stands on its own. It is a full paragraph; a deck — a subtitle or one-line teaser — is a different device and no substitute for it.\\n\\n- One paragraph, at most 60 words.\\n- Self-contained: a reader who sees only the standfirst understands it fully.\\n- It says what the article is about and what the reader gains from reading it.\\n- It entices the reader to read on.\\n\\n## Byline\\n\\nOne line naming the author. In English: `By <author>`. In another language, that language's conventional byline form. The author is the one the brief names; when it names none, the author is the user.\\n\\n## Lead\\n\\nThe lead is the first paragraph of the body: one paragraph, meeting every requirement under *Paragraphs*. It introduces the subject and gives the reader a clear expectation of what they will learn by reading on.\\n\\n### Standfirst and lead\\n\\nBoth open the text. Each works without the other, and read together they never repeat:\\n\\n- The body reads complete without the standfirst, which is often shown apart from the body or skipped. The lead introduces every person, thing and event it mentions, and nothing in the body points back to the standfirst.\\n- They should begin with different first words, and an everyday word such as *the* or *it* counts like any other.\\n- Read in sequence, the lead advances. A reader who has just read the standfirst meets the lead as new material, never as the standfirst said again; what the body needs from the standfirst is named again in passing, and the lead moves on.\\n\\n## Paragraphs\\n\\n- Each paragraph holds one thought, and the thought sets its length. Most paragraphs come to two or three sentences. A thought that takes one sentence, or four or five, gets exactly that.\\n- A break may also fall for drama: a single sentence, or a single word, stands alone when isolating it creates an effect the text needs.\\n- Sentence length varies within the paragraph, giving the text a natural rhythm.\\n- A paragraph should be at most 80 words, so that a web reader takes it in at a glance. Eighty is the outer edge; aim well below it.\\n\\n## Subheadings and sections\\n\\nA section is a subheading plus the paragraphs under it.\\n\\n- A subheading is at most 70 characters, spaces included.\\n- It describes clearly what the paragraphs under it are about, and it is written as [Headlines](headlines.md) says.\\n- A section holds at least one paragraph and should hold at most three. Most sections hold two or three.\\n\\n## Ending\\n\\nThe ending is a section of its own, opened by its own subheading, after at least one other section. It is the last section, and it is short: one paragraph can be enough. It does two things:\\n\\n- It shows that the expectation the lead set has been met.\\n- It calls the reader to action, and the action follows from the article's content. The selected genre says what that action is in its kind of text.\\n\\nThe last section is the ending when its subheading and paragraphs close the piece — the call to action, the reflection, the recommendation or the next step — and it opens no new line of argument. Stating the recommendation or proposal the text has built towards is closing content, even where the last section is the first place the text states it in full; so are the writer's own reservation about it and the call to try or adopt it. What makes a section carry the argument is reasons or evidence the conclusion rests on that no earlier section gave. A last section that states the proposal, admits a doubt about it and asks the reader to act on it brings none, and is the ending. A section that carries the argument with a closing line appended is not an ending.\\n# Headlines\\n\\nHow a headline is written, in any genre that links this file. A headline lets the reader judge at once whether the text is for them: it states the core of the text as briefly and precisely as it can, and it is understood on its own, with no image, standfirst or surrounding page to explain it. *Headline* here covers a text's subheadings too; for a subheading, the text is its section.\\n\\n## Statement or label\\n\\nA text that reports, explains, argues, decides or narrates gets a statement headline: a clause that says what the text says. A text the reader looks things up in or navigates by — a policy, a manual, opening hours, a contact page — gets a plain label naming what it holds: *Privacy policy*, where a statement would read *How we protect your data*. Everything below is about the statement headline.\\n\\n## What a statement headline does\\n\\n- It states the angle: the single most important message, the sentence that would remain if only one could. A subject alone is a label. Test: the headline could not head a different text on the same subject.\\n- It is a simple clause — subject, active verb, object where there is one — in straight word order, and in the present tense where that fits: *Council introduces free bus travel*.\\n- The most important keyword comes first.\\n- A concrete detail stands in it when the detail carries the message: a number, a date, a place. *Three schools to close this autumn* beats *Changes to school organisation*.\\n- The reader sees what they gain: a decision, a fact, a change. An instructional text may address the reader directly (*you*, *your*) and open on *How to*, *Why* or *Here's*.\\n- It is as short as the angle allows. Modifiers, repetition and filler go; a keyword that carries meaning stays. The format the text follows sets the limits.\\n- It sounds like something a person would say aloud.\\n- Its words are ones this audience knows. A technical term is the right keyword for specialists and noise for a general reader, and the same holds for names and abbreviations.\\n- Its tone is the text's tone, and never louder. Illness, accidents, crime and crises get a sober, understated headline; a light text can carry more personality.\\n- It is plain before it is clever. It works in full for a reader who misses every allusion, so a pun or a reference never carries the meaning.\\n- It gives the answer. Journalists hold a question headline to be the wrong form, and so does this: it is a deliberate exception, natural over a text built as questions and answers, and available where the text truly leaves the question open and the headline is meant to assert nothing.\\n- The verb does the work. A colon standing in for a verb, a clipped quotation and headline-speak all give way to the full clause.\\n- It complements what follows. Headline and standfirst, or subheading and the first sentence under it, say different things in different words.\\n\\n## Every word supported\\n\\nA headline claims only what its text claims. Every figure, name, claim and conclusion in it is in the text, at the text's own strength: never sharper, never more general, never a conclusion the text does not draw. A reader who finishes the text finds that the headline told the truth about it.\\n\\n## A subheading over a quotation\\n\\nA quotation in a section is there to carry what only its speaker can say: a judgement, a figure, a concession. A subheading standing over it, however far down the section the quotation stands, leaves that to the quotation. It states the section's angle from what the rest of the section says, or, where the quotation is the section's point, names the subject, the occasion or the speaker the quotation speaks to, and not the point itself. Test: a reader who has just read the subheading meets the quotation as new material, never as the subheading said again. *The clinic would double its training time*, over a head nurse saying *Next time I would double the training time before we switch*, has spent the quotation before the reader reaches it; *The head nurse looks back on the switch* leaves it to her.\\n\\nThe quotation bridge is the narrative sentence or clause that stands immediately before a quotation and leads the reader into it, including a speech tag that carries anything besides the attribution. The genre that quotes says what a quotation bridge may carry. A subheading standing over a quotation is outside the quotation bridge and is governed by this section.\\n\\n## Getting there\\n\\nSettle the reader and the angle before wording anything. Then write three to five variants and keep the clearest, which is rarely the cleverest. Word a subheading from its whole section, once the section is written, in words its first sentence does not use, and leave a quotation standing under it the judgement, figure or concession it is there to carry.\\n\"}}}"}, {"type": "input_text", "text": "{\"read\":2,\"result\":{\"status\":\"fulfilled\",\"value\":{\"chunk_id\":\"e8946f\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000001916,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":79,\"output\":\"total 0\\ndrwxr-xr-x@ 6 thomas  staff  192 Oct  1 14:38 .\\ndrwx------@ 6 thomas  staff  192 Oct  1 14:37 ..\\ndrwxr-xr-x@ 2 thomas  staff   64 Oct  1 14:37 cache\\ndrwxr-xr-x@ 2 thomas  staff   64 Oct  1 14:37 data\\ndrwxr-xr-x@ 2 thomas  staff   64 Oct  1 14:37 tmp\\ndrwxr-xr-x@ 2 thomas  staff   64 Oct  1 14:37 uv-cache\\n\"}}}"}], "internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough": {"turn_id": "01a0f778-385b-76c1-b8f2-0236f45872a7", "create_time": 1790858314.280486}}}
{"session": "01a0f778-36c8-7653-b4e1-24cd2500f495", "timestamp": "2026-10-01T12:41:08.568Z", "call": {"type": "custom_tool_call", "id": "ctc_091db10e91878489016abe54cc7d2087d2bc77b21457f689a5", "status": "completed", "call_id": "call_10z2fxg9QYvhTmqfLhmrXE0n", "name": "exec", "input": "const draft = \"# Lervik should test before ending telephone bookings\\n\\nBooking totals in Lervik’s pilot report do not measure residents’ ability to book online. The municipal executive board must weigh staff costs against the value of telephone access, yet the documents contain neither a time measurement nor a calculation of financial savings.\\n\\nBy Sanna Ek, spokesperson for Öppna beslut\\n\\nThe officer’s report for Lervik’s municipal executive board meeting on 18 June proposes removing telephone booking from all seven venues for local associations from September. I do not oppose digital booking. But on behalf of Öppna beslut, I urge the board to postpone a permanent switch and test telephone and web booking together for six months.\\n\\n## Bookings tell us too little about users\\n\\nThe municipality’s pilot report, dated 8 April 2026, records 96 web bookings and 24 telephone bookings during an eight-week trial at two venues. Those are bookings, not unique people. The report measures neither age nor functional ability nor familiarity with digital technology.\\n\\nSo the telephone figure cannot tell us what proportion of Lervik’s residents cannot book online. Nor does choosing the telephone prove an inability to use the web. It gives us a reason to ask why that route is still used.\\n\\n## Staff time needs a fair comparison\\n\\nThe administration’s objection to keeping both routes is the administrative work across two channels: staff enter information in two workflows. That concern deserves to be taken seriously. But the documents provide no measurement of the time involved or calculation of financial savings.\\n\\nRemoving a route before establishing its value to users and the work it requires leaves the board without the comparison it needs. Administrative simplicity is a reason to investigate the balance, not a substitute for doing so.\\n\\n## A wider trial can inform the choice\\n\\nÖppna beslut proposes keeping both booking routes for six months at all seven venues. During that trial, the administration should record the time spent on each route and ask users to say voluntarily why they choose telephone or web booking.\\n\\nWe make no claim to have financed or costed that trial. The executive board must consider its cost. Asking for better evidence does not remove the need to decide what gathering it is worth.\\n\\n## The board can make its lasting choice later\\n\\nLervik’s municipal executive board should now postpone the permanent switch and agree a six-month trial of both routes, with its cost explicitly considered. After that, it can decide whether to remove, change or retain a booking channel.\\n\";\nstore(\"write_draft\",draft);\ntext(await tools.exec_command({\ncmd:\"python3 - <<'PY'\\nimport os\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\nimport subprocess\\nimport tempfile\\nscratch_root = Path('/private/var/folders/cs/vgp_x_353zd9frkjpysp7zdw0000gn/T/kntnt-native-session-r95dqkak/scratch')\\ncheck_dir = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix='write-source-check-', dir=scratch_root))\\ndraft = \" + JSON.stringify(draft) + \"\\n(check_dir / 'draft.md').write_text(draft, encoding='utf-8')\\nprint('SOURCE_CHECK_DIRECTORY=' + str(check_dir), flush=True)\\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix='write-measure-uv-', dir=scratch_root) as run_tmp:\\n    env = dict(os.environ, TMPDIR=run_tmp, UV_NO_CACHE='1', UV_NO_PROJECT='1')\\n    result = subprocess.run(['uv', 'run', '--no-cache', '--no-project', '/private/var/folders/cs/vgp_x_353zd9frkjpysp7zdw0000gn/T/kntnt-native-session-r95dqkak/work/.agents/skills/kntnt/library/scripts/article_anatomy.py', '-'], input=draft, text=True, env=env)\\nraise SystemExit(result.returncode)\\nPY\",\nmax_output_tokens:8000\n}));\n", "internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough": {"turn_id": "01a0f778-385b-76c1-b8f2-0236f45872a7", "create_time": 1790858315.053618}}}

{"session": "01a0f778-36c8-7653-b4e1-24cd2500f495", "timestamp": "2026-10-01T12:56:41.336Z", "result": {"type": "custom_tool_call_output", "id": "ctco_01a0f789-d878-73b2-b2a9-5fb66128f2cb", "call_id": "call_SEWphGa3o5yjAXfEQNrEd1Cb", "output": [{"type": "input_text", "text": "Script completed\nWall time 0.1 seconds\nOutput:\n"}, {"type": "input_text", "text": "{\"chunk_id\":\"beca02\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000001834,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":5533,\"output\":\"# Complete source and quotation comparison\\n\\nExamined the full Swedish source.md and the full British English draft.md, including all headline, standfirst, byline, subheadings and body passages. The only factual source is the supplied fictional Lervik brief. No direct speech quotation occurs in the draft. No sources or draft were changed.\\n\\n## Claim accounting\\n\\nFor each item below, quoted source wording retains the qualifications needed for the comparison. Authorial arguments are identified separately from claims about facts.\\n\\n1. **Headline: “Lervik should test before ending telephone bookings”.**\\n   Source: “Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb.”\\n   This is the commissioned recommendation. The municipal place name stands for its decision-making body. “Before” retains the order; the body supplies the trial length and permanent-change qualification. It does not say telephone booking has already ended. Supported advocacy.\\n\\n2. **Standfirst: “Booking totals in Lervik’s pilot report do not measure residents’ ability to book online.”**\\n   Source: “Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer. Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana. Telefonbokningarna kan därför inte användas för att säga hur stor andel av invånarna som inte kan boka digitalt.”\\n   The negative concerns what booking totals measure, not every conceivable item elsewhere in the report. It does not assert that residents can book online or that callers have no difficulties. Supported explanation of the count’s limits.\\n\\n3. **Standfirst: “The municipal executive board must weigh staff costs against the value of telephone access”.**\\n   Source: “Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna.”\\n   Context: “Att en bokning går via telefon är inte ett bevis för att någon inte kan använda webben. Det är ett skäl att ta reda på varför telefonen fortfarande används.”\\n   The byline and first-person body preserve Sanna’s ownership of this position. This is advocacy, not an asserted legal duty. Focusing user value on the route proposed for removal is supported argument about that proposal, not an invented measured benefit. Staff costs renders “arbetskostnaden”; the board is the decision-maker identified in the brief.\\n\\n4. **Standfirst: “yet the documents contain neither a time measurement nor a calculation of financial savings.”**\\n   Source: “Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna.”\\n   The documentary scope and both absences match. “Financial savings” is idiomatic for the supplied economic savings calculation in this staff-cost context. No claim that savings are impossible is added.\\n\\n5. **Byline: “By Sanna Ek, spokesperson for Öppna beslut”.**\\n   Source: “Avsändare är Sanna Ek, talesperson för föreningen Öppna beslut.”\\n   Author and role match; the byline does not identify the user. English “spokesperson” asserts no natural gender.\\n\\n6. **Opening: “The officer’s report for Lervik’s municipal executive board meeting on 18 June”.**\\n   Source: “Tjänsteutlåtandet inför kommunstyrelsens möte den 18 juni”\\n   Body, meeting and date match; no meeting year has been introduced. “For” retains the report’s connection with the forthcoming meeting. “The officer’s” adds singular officer provenance not supplied by the document designation. See SS-1.\\n\\n7. **Opening: “proposes removing telephone booking from all seven venues for local associations from September.”**\\n   Source: “föreslår att telefonbokning tas bort för alla sju lokaler från september.”\\n   Defining context: “ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler”; “Kommunens pilotrapport Bokning av föreningslokaler”.\\n   Proposed status, telephone route, all seven premises and September match. No adopted decision is invented. “Venues” is compatible with the bookable premises in this context. “Local associations” adds a geographical restriction to the associations. See SS-2.\\n\\n8. **Opening: “I do not oppose digital booking.”**\\n   Source: “Hon motsätter sig inte digital bokning.”\\n   The authorised first-person author is Sanna. The stance matches and does not imply support for every online-only proposal.\\n\\n9. **Opening: “But on behalf of Öppna beslut, I urge the board to postpone a permanent switch and test telephone and web booking together for six months.”**\\n   Source: “Avsändare är Sanna Ek, talesperson för föreningen Öppna beslut. Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb.”\\n   Further source: “Öppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar.”\\n   Representative capacity, requested postponement, permanence, both routes and duration match. The previous sentence identifies the switch. This does not report approval or financing.\\n\\n10. **Subheading: “Bookings tell us too little about users”.**\\n    Source: “Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer. Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana. Telefonbokningarna kan därför inte användas för att säga hur stor andel av invånarna som inte kan boka digitalt.”\\n    “Too little” is the author’s evaluation against the information needed for this decision. It does not characterise the count of 24 as high or low. The following prose specifies the information limits. Supported argument.\\n\\n11. **Pilot paragraph: “The municipality’s pilot report, dated 8 April 2026, records 96 web bookings and 24 telephone bookings during an eight-week trial at two venues.”**\\n    Source: “Kommunens pilotrapport Bokning av föreningslokaler, 8 april 2026, gäller ett åtta veckor långt försök i två lokaler. Under försöket gjordes 96 bokningar via webb och 24 via telefon.”\\n    Report attribution, date, counts, booking unit, channels, eight weeks and two premises match. The figures are explicitly attributed where they support the argument. Omission of the report’s title does not introduce a different title.\\n\\n12. **“Those are bookings, not unique people.”**\\n    Source: “Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer.”\\n    Same unit and exclusion; unique people are not counted or estimated.\\n\\n13. **“The report measures neither age nor functional ability nor familiarity with digital technology.”**\\n    Source: “Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana.”\\n    All three exclusions match. In this user-access context, “functional ability” denotes personal functional capacity, and “familiarity with digital technology” denotes practical digital familiarity. The latter is not read here as academic knowledge about technology. No concrete contextual obstruction, diagnosis or population figure has been added.\\n\\n14. **“So the telephone figure cannot tell us what proportion of Lervik’s residents cannot book online.”**\\n    Source: “Telefonbokningarna kan därför inte användas för att säga hur stor andel av invånarna som inte kan boka digitalt.”\\n    Population scope, inference limit, inability and channel match. Unknown inability has not been converted into proven ability.\\n\\n15. **“Nor does choosing the telephone prove an inability to use the web.”**\\n    Source: “Sanna Eks ståndpunkt: ‘Att en bokning går via telefon är inte ett bevis för att någon inte kan använda webben.’”\\n    The stance is Sanna’s, indirectly rendered without quotation marks. “Choosing” also has support in “varför de väljer telefon eller webb”; it does not assert unconstrained choices or no barriers. No individual’s motive or ability is invented.\\n\\n16. **“It gives us a reason to ask why that route is still used.”**\\n    Source: “Det är ett skäl att ta reda på varför telefonen fortfarande används.”\\n    Telephone use supplies a reason to investigate. Continued use matches; no reason for that use is asserted as known.\\n\\n17. **Subheading: “Staff time needs a fair comparison”.**\\n    Source: “Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg”; “Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna.”\\n    Supported authorial evaluation of the proposed comparison, without asserting an existing measured comparison or quantified unfairness.\\n\\n18. **“The administration’s objection to keeping both routes is the administrative work across two channels: staff enter information in two workflows.”**\\n    Source: “Motivet i utlåtandet är att personalen ska slippa föra in uppgifter i två flöden. Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna. Förvaltningens verkliga invändning mot att behålla båda kanalerna är dubbel administration, inte att telefonanvändare är lata eller dyra.”\\n    Actual supplied objection and operational explanation match. Avoiding entry in two workflows supports the work being avoided. There is no time amount, cost amount, laziness, party motive or causal savings claim.\\n\\n19. **“That concern deserves to be taken seriously.”**\\n    Source: “Förvaltningens verkliga invändning mot att behålla båda kanalerna är dubbel administration”; “Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna.”\\n    The author assesses a supported concern; no view is attributed to a new person. Supported argument.\\n\\n20. **“But the documents provide no measurement of the time involved or calculation of financial savings.”**\\n    Source: “Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna.”\\n    Same documentary scope and absences. The preceding workflow clause settles “time involved”. No absence outside the documents is claimed.\\n\\n21. **“Removing a route before establishing its value to users and the work it requires leaves the board without the comparison it needs.”**\\n    Source: “Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna.”\\n    Factual premise: “Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna.”\\n    Proposed investigation: “Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg och be användarna frivilligt ange varför de väljer telefon eller webb.”\\n    This is conditional authorial reasoning about removal before the relevant comparison. It does not report an actual removal, a quantified causal effect or the absence of every form of user information everywhere.\\n\\n22. **“Administrative simplicity is a reason to investigate the balance, not a substitute for doing so.”**\\n    Source: “Motivet i utlåtandet är att personalen ska slippa föra in uppgifter i två flöden”; “Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna.”\\n    Characterising avoidance of double administration as an aim of simplicity is supported. The remaining clause is Sanna’s argument, not a measured technical effect or consensus.\\n\\n23. **Subheading: “A wider trial can inform the choice”.**\\n    Source: “ett åtta veckor långt försök i två lokaler”; “Öppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar. Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg och be användarna frivilligt ange varför de väljer telefon eller webb”; “Därefter kan den avgöra om en kanal ska tas bort, ändras eller behållas.”\\n    “Wider” has a comparison: seven premises rather than two, plus a longer period. “Can inform” states the proposed evidence-gathering purpose; it guarantees no outcome.\\n\\n24. **“Öppna beslut proposes keeping both booking routes for six months at all seven venues.”**\\n    Source: “Öppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar.”\\n    Proposer, proposed status, two routes, duration and coverage match. Subheading and next sentence retain trial status.\\n\\n25. **“During that trial, the administration should record the time spent on each route and ask users to say voluntarily why they choose telephone or web booking.”**\\n    Source: “Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg och be användarna frivilligt ange varför de väljer telefon eller webb.”\\n    Actor, proposed trial, measurement per route, voluntary user disclosure and options match. “Should” is recommendation, not a report of an adopted order. No additional user variables are introduced.\\n\\n26. **“We make no claim to have financed or costed that trial.”**\\n    Source: “Föreningen gör inget anspråk på att ha finansierat eller kostnadsberäknat försöket.”\\n    The institutional speaker is the preceding Öppna beslut. No claim of financing or costing remains distinct from knowing that neither happened. British “costed” means having calculated costs. Supported.\\n\\n27. **“The executive board must consider its cost.”**\\n    Source: “Kostnaden behöver kommunstyrelsen ta ställning till.”\\n    Body and trial cost match. “Consider” is less explicit in isolation than taking a position, but the next sentence supplies deciding what evidence-gathering is worth and the final recommendation puts cost into the board’s trial decision. In context the cost judgement and uncertainty are preserved.\\n\\n28. **“Asking for better evidence does not remove the need to decide what gathering it is worth.”**\\n    Source: “Föreningen gör inget anspråk på att ha finansierat eller kostnadsberäknat försöket. Kostnaden behöver kommunstyrelsen ta ställning till.”\\n    Supported authorial reflection about cost responsibility. No amount, available budget or affordability conclusion is given.\\n\\n29. **Subheading: “Evidence should guide the final booking decision”.**\\n    Source: “Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb”; “Därefter kan den avgöra om en kanal ska tas bort, ändras eller behållas.”\\n    Supported commissioned position. “Final” distinguishes permanent channel choice from trial; it does not assert legal irreversibility.\\n\\n30. **“Lervik’s municipal executive board should now postpone the permanent switch and agree a six-month trial of both routes, with its cost explicitly considered.”**\\n    Source: “Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb”; “Öppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar”; “Föreningen gör inget anspråk på att ha finansierat eller kostnadsberäknat försöket. Kostnaden behöver kommunstyrelsen ta ställning till.”\\n    Actor, recommendation, permanence, period and two routes match. “Now” is advocated timing, not an event. “Agree” is requested, not reported approval. Explicit cost consideration plus the preceding no-claim statement preserves uncertainty.\\n\\n31. **“After that, it can decide whether to remove, change or retain a booking channel.”**\\n    Source: “Kostnaden behöver kommunstyrelsen ta ställning till. Därefter kan den avgöra om en kanal ska tas bort, ändras eller behållas.”\\n    The board, post-trial and cost-consideration sequence, modality and three possible decisions match. Retention is not presented as the only acceptable result.\\n\\n## Separate pronoun accounting\\n\\n- “I” in the two opening uses: first-person singular, natural gender unspecified. Authorship and the position are supplied. Source “Hon” is third-person singular and naturally female; the authorised authorial change adds no gender claim.\\n- “Those” in “Those are bookings”: plural demonstrative, natural gender unspecified; the supplied counts and “bokningar” establish plurality.\\n- “us” in the first subheading, population-inference sentence and investigation sentence: first-person plural, natural gender unspecified; inclusive author/reader rhetoric for an article to residents. It neither counts a population nor attributes Sanna’s view to every resident.\\n- “It” in “It gives us a reason”: third-person singular, natural gender unspecified, a reference to telephone use as a fact. Swedish “Det” likewise denotes that fact.\\n- “that route”, “both routes”, “each route” and “both booking routes”: singular or distributed/plural as stated, natural gender unspecified; the source expressly supplies telephone and web routes.\\n- “That concern”: singular demonstrative, natural gender unspecified; the supplied double-administration objection.\\n- “its” in “its value to users” and “it” in “the work it requires”: third-person singular, natural gender unspecified; a hypothetical route in authorial reasoning. They add no measured value or work amount.\\n- “it” in “the comparison it needs”: third-person singular, natural gender unspecified; the board in Sanna’s argument. It does not assert that the board itself has publicly adopted her assessment.\\n- “doing so”: refers to investigating the balance; no person, number or natural-gender claim.\\n- “they” in “why they choose”: third-person plural, natural gender unspecified; source “användarna” and “de väljer” establish plural users without male/female composition.\\n- “We” in “We make no claim”: first-person plural, natural gender unspecified; organisational voice through its spokesperson. Source “Föreningen” supplies the asserting party, not a specific count of authors, staff or participants.\\n- “its” in the two trial-cost passages: third-person singular, natural gender unspecified; source “Kostnaden” in the trial paragraph supplies the trial-cost relationship.\\n- “it” in “gathering it”: third-person singular, natural gender unspecified; evidence in the author’s cost reflection.\\n- “that” in “After that”: singular demonstrative, natural gender unspecified; the proposed trial/consideration sequence.\\n- Final “it can decide”: third-person singular, natural gender unspecified; source “den” refers to “kommunstyrelsen”. Swedish grammatical common gender does not assert a human’s natural gender.\\n\\nNo additional human gender, personal experience, individual user or speaker is introduced through these pronouns.\\n\\n## Source-support findings\\n\\n### SS-1: singular officer provenance\\n\\nDraft: “The officer’s report for Lervik’s municipal executive board meeting on 18 June”.\\n\\nExact source: “Tjänsteutlåtandet inför kommunstyrelsens möte den 18 juni”.\\n\\nThe source names an administrative document, without a singular officer or its authorship. The draft’s singular possessive adds that provenance.\\n\\nTwo-direction test:\\n- A municipal department could issue the tjänsteutlåtande jointly through several officials, with no single officer owning or authoring it. The supplied material names the administration and document, but contains no statement excluding that case. The source’s designation holds; the singular officer provenance does not.\\n- Conversely, a single officer could produce a supporting report for the meeting that is not the formal tjänsteutlåtande. The draft’s generic description could hold while the specific source-document designation does not. No supplied statement equates every officer report with that formal document.\\n\\nSmallest supported repair: replace “The officer’s report” with “The administrative report”. Preserve its meeting, dates, proposal and other substantive details.\\n\\n### SS-2: geographical restriction on associations\\n\\nDraft: “all seven venues for local associations”.\\n\\nExact source: “alla sju lokaler”; the premises are defined by “bokning av föreningslokaler”.\\n\\nThe source does not restrict associations to being locally based. Local audience and municipal setting do not supply that restriction.\\n\\nTwo-direction test:\\n- A nationwide association could use one of these association premises. The supplied “föreningslokaler” description remains true, but the restriction to “local associations” need not hold. No supplied statement excludes national associations or their bookings.\\n- Every venue for local associations is also a venue for associations; there is no reverse counterexample on this restriction. The asymmetry shows that the draft narrows the source.\\n\\nSmallest supported repair: delete “local”: “all seven venues for associations”.\\n\\nBoth findings concern source support in translated descriptive prose, rather than translation of quoted speech.\\n\\n## Quotation and boundary results\\n\\nThere are no direct speech quotations, so no quotation-translation idiom or distinctive-voice findings. Sanna’s indirectly rendered supplied statement preserves its meaning and attribution.\\n\\nNo unsupported legal requirement, protest, discrimination event, party motive, savings amount, causal saving effect, telephone-user motive or personal experience appears. Figures remain attributed to the pilot report. The no-financing/no-costing-claim wording preserves the state of knowledge. Trial cost uncertainty and the board’s three possible final channel decisions remain explicit. The author’s rhetorical evaluations and recommendations are supported as her position rather than externally proved facts.\\n\\n## Completion status\\n\\nComparison complete. Source and draft unchanged. Two unresolved source-support findings remain in the current draft: SS-1, singular officer provenance; SS-2, “local associations”. No quotation-translation findings.\\n\"}"}], "internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough": {"turn_id": "01a0f778-385b-76c1-b8f2-0236f45872a7", "create_time": 1790859401.336231}}}

{"session": "01a0f778-36c8-7653-b4e1-24cd2500f495", "timestamp": "2026-10-01T13:07:32.737Z", "call": {"type": "custom_tool_call", "id": "ctc_091db10e91878489016abe5b12bee887d2b5891907fd1072e1", "status": "completed", "call_id": "call_F1X95KVO4gtI8hbgm3u64SC6", "name": "exec", "input": "text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:\"cat /private/var/folders/cs/vgp_x_353zd9frkjpysp7zdw0000gn/T/kntnt-native-session-r95dqkak/scratch/write-source-check-phwbhm4c/report-2.md\",max_output_tokens:18000}));\n", "internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough": {"turn_id": "01a0f778-385b-76c1-b8f2-0236f45872a7", "create_time": 1790860048.157712}}}
{"session": "01a0f778-36c8-7653-b4e1-24cd2500f495", "timestamp": "2026-10-01T13:07:32.832Z", "result": {"type": "custom_tool_call_output", "id": "ctco_01a0f793-c960-7be1-91c6-67c196e55e33", "call_id": "call_F1X95KVO4gtI8hbgm3u64SC6", "output": [{"type": "input_text", "text": "Script completed\nWall time 0.0 seconds\nOutput:\n"}, {"type": "input_text", "text": "{\"chunk_id\":\"5174c2\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.00000175,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":4773,\"output\":\"# Source and quotation comparison\\n\\nRead `source.md` and the complete supplied `draft.md`, including headline, standfirst, byline and subheadings. Source language: Swedish. Target language: British English. This report concerns source support only. The byline identifies the source brief’s author, not the user.\\n\\n## Complete claim accounting\\n\\n| Draft passage | Source passage, in its original language | Comparison |\\n| --- | --- | --- |\\n| “Lervik should test before ending telephone bookings” | “Skriv en debattartikel för invånarna i fiktiva Lervik. Avsändare är Sanna Ek, talesperson för föreningen Öppna beslut. Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb.” | Supported advocacy. The title retains testing before removal; the body supplies the permanence, duration, board and two-channel qualifications. |\\n| “Booking totals in Lervik’s pilot report do not measure residents’ ability to book online.” | “Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer. Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana. Telefonbokningarna kan därför inte användas för att säga hur stor andel av invånarna som inte kan boka digitalt.” | Supported contextual conclusion about what the totals measure. No individual or population inability is inferred. The report is the named source. |\\n| “The municipal executive board must weigh staff costs against the value of telephone access” | “Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna.” | Supported as Sanna’s own advocacy in her article. “Must weigh” states her requested decision standard; it does not assert that a comparison has occurred. Telephone access identifies the retained channel whose user value is at issue in the brief. |\\n| “yet the documents contain neither a time measurement nor a calculation of financial savings.” | “Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna.” | Same document scope and absence of the specified measurements. The draft does not convert missing calculations into zero savings. |\\n| “By Sanna Ek, spokesperson for Öppna beslut” | “Avsändare är Sanna Ek, talesperson för föreningen Öppna beslut.” | Name and role match. “Spokesperson” adds no natural-gender assertion. |\\n| “The administrative report for Lervik’s municipal executive board meeting on 18 June proposes removing telephone booking from all seven venues for associations from September.” | “Tjänsteutlåtandet inför kommunstyrelsens möte den 18 juni föreslår att telefonbokning tas bort för alla sju lokaler från september.” Also: “ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler” | The same report is identified by its meeting and proposal. Date, proposal modality, seven-premises scope and September commencement match. “Administrative report” is a generic English description of the supplied document; the identifying details prevent substitution of a different report. “Venues for associations” identifies the supplied bookable association premises in this context, without asserting any additional event, size or ownership. |\\n| “I do not oppose digital booking.” | “Hon motsätter sig inte digital bokning.” | Supported. Third-person reporting becomes the commissioned author’s first-person statement. No stronger endorsement is claimed. |\\n| “But on behalf of Öppna beslut, I urge the board to postpone a permanent switch and test telephone and web booking together for six months.” | “Avsändare är Sanna Ek, talesperson för föreningen Öppna beslut. Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb.” Also: “Öppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar.” | Author, representative capacity, requested board action, permanence, both channels and six-month duration are supported. A proposal remains a proposal. |\\n| “Bookings tell us too little about users” | “Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer. Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana. Telefonbokningarna kan därför inte användas för att säga hur stor andel av invånarna som inte kan boka digitalt.” | Supported author evaluation of the evidence’s usefulness for this decision. It does not characterise 24 or 96 as a high or low count. |\\n| “The municipality’s pilot report, dated 8 April 2026, records 96 web bookings and 24 telephone bookings during an eight-week trial at two venues.” | “Kommunens pilotrapport Bokning av föreningslokaler, 8 april 2026, gäller ett åtta veckor långt försök i två lokaler. Under försöket gjordes 96 bokningar via webb och 24 via telefon.” | Correct named attribution, date, duration, two-premises scope, route and counts. No people count, unique-user count, causal effect or extrapolation is added. |\\n| “Those are bookings, not unique people.” | “Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer.” | Exact unit and exclusion preserved. |\\n| “The report measures neither age nor functional ability nor familiarity with digital technology.” | “Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana.” | Age and functional ability match. “Familiarity with digital technology” can cover knowledge without practical accustomedness, broadening the third measurement exclusion. Source-support finding F1 below. |\\n| “So the telephone figure cannot tell us what proportion of Lervik’s residents cannot book online.” | “Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana. Telefonbokningarna kan därför inte användas för att säga hur stor andel av invånarna som inte kan boka digitalt.” | The logical qualification “därför” and population scope are preserved. “Cannot” is warranted, rather than weakened to an unsupported possibility. |\\n| “Nor does choosing the telephone prove an inability to use the web. It gives us a reason to ask why that route is still used.” | “Sanna Eks ståndpunkt: ”Att en bokning går via telefon är inte ett bevis för att någon inte kan använda webben. Det är ett skäl att ta reda på varför telefonen fortfarande används.” Detta får citeras eller refereras.” | Supported paraphrase of the author’s stated position. “Choosing” is used in a general evidential proposition; it does not describe a particular person’s choice or invent a reason for it. The claim that a telephone booking does not establish inability also supports the narrower proposition about a chosen telephone booking. Continued telephone use is in the source. |\\n| “Staff time needs a fair comparison” | “Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna.” Also: “Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna.” | Supported normative heading. “Fair” describes the author’s desired comparison, not a measured result or a claim of dishonest conduct. |\\n| “The administration’s objection to keeping both routes is the administrative work across two channels: staff enter information in two workflows.” | “Motivet i utlåtandet är att personalen ska slippa föra in uppgifter i två flöden. Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna. Förvaltningens verkliga invändning mot att behålla båda kanalerna är dubbel administration, inte att telefonanvändare är lata eller dyra.” | The objection and its explanation remain attributed to the administration in this sentence. The colon explains that stated objection; it is not a separately sourced observation of staff work. Both workflows and double administration are supplied. No quantified workload, saving, laziness or expense attributed to users is added. |\\n| “That concern deserves to be taken seriously. But the documents provide no measurement of the time involved or calculation of financial savings.” | “Förvaltningens verkliga invändning mot att behålla båda kanalerna är dubbel administration, inte att telefonanvändare är lata eller dyra.” Also: “Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna.” | First sentence is the author’s evaluation of the supported objection. Second preserves the documentary limitation, without claiming there is no administrative cost or saving. |\\n| “Removing a route before establishing its value to users and the work it requires leaves the board without the comparison it needs. Administrative simplicity is a reason to investigate the balance, not a substitute for doing so.” | “Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna.” Also: “Motivet i utlåtandet är att personalen ska slippa föra in uppgifter i två flöden. Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna.” | Author’s argument about the requirements of a decision and an expressly conditional sequence. It does not claim a trial has proved a benefit, assign a fabricated motive, or assert a measured technical effect. The comparison and the stated administrative concern have source support. |\\n| “A wider trial can inform the choice” | “Kommunens pilotrapport Bokning av föreningslokaler, 8 april 2026, gäller ett åtta veckor långt försök i två lokaler.” “Öppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar.” Also: “Därefter kan den avgöra om en kanal ska tas bort, ändras eller behållas.” | “Wider” has the explicit two-versus-seven scope comparison. “Can inform” expresses the supported proposal’s purpose and possibility; it guarantees no result. |\\n| “Öppna beslut proposes keeping both booking routes for six months at all seven venues.” | “Öppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar.” | Proposer, proposal status, both channels, duration and full seven-premises scope match. |\\n| “During that trial, the administration should record the time spent on each route and ask users to say voluntarily why they choose telephone or web booking.” | “Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg och be användarna frivilligt ange varför de väljer telefon eller webb.” | Proposed administration action, timing, per-route unit, users’ voluntary participation, and question about reasons all match. “Should” fits the proposal and does not imply an enacted instruction or future certainty. |\\n| “We make no claim to have financed or costed that trial.” | “Föreningen gör inget anspråk på att ha finansierat eller kostnadsberäknat försöket.” | Exact epistemic limitation preserved. The draft does not claim that financing or costing has not happened. |\\n| “The executive board must consider its cost. Asking for better evidence does not remove the need to decide what gathering it is worth.” | “Kostnaden behöver kommunstyrelsen ta ställning till.” | First sentence preserves the board’s responsibility and unresolved cost. Second is the author’s reflection on that requirement; it supplies no price, affordability finding, funding commitment or estimated saving. |\\n| “Evidence should guide the final booking decision” | “Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna.” Also: “Därefter kan den avgöra om en kanal ska tas bort, ändras eller behållas.” | Supported author advocacy about the decision standard. No completed final decision is asserted. |\\n| “Lervik’s municipal executive board should now postpone the permanent switch and agree a six-month trial of both routes, with its cost explicitly considered.” | “Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb.” Also: “Öppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar.” And: “Kostnaden behöver kommunstyrelsen ta ställning till.” | Supported present advocacy, naming the board’s possible decision and retaining cost uncertainty. “Now” is the article’s appeal at the proposed decision point, not a new dated event. |\\n| “After that, it can decide whether to remove, change or retain a booking channel.” | “Kostnaden behöver kommunstyrelsen ta ställning till. Därefter kan den avgöra om en kanal ska tas bort, ändras eller behållas.” | Board referent, subsequent decision, possibility rather than promised outcome, singular channel and all three options are preserved. In context the source’s proposed sequence includes the trial and consideration of cost; the preceding draft sentence likewise preserves both. |\\n\\n## Pronouns, person, number and natural gender\\n\\n| Draft form | Account |\\n| --- | --- |\\n| “I” in the two first-person author statements | First person, singular, no natural gender. Referent is Sanna Ek under “Avsändare är Sanna Ek” and the byline. This is the commissioned author’s voice. Source “Hon motsätter sig inte digital bokning” explicitly attributes the position to her; the English first-person form does not reproduce or add a third-person gender assertion. |\\n| “We” in “We make no claim” | First person, plural, no natural gender. Organisational voice on behalf of Öppna beslut, supported by the spokesperson brief and “Föreningen gör inget anspråk …”. It expresses the association’s position, not an invented assertion about the user or a counted membership. |\\n| “us” in the heading and explanatory argument | First person, plural, no natural gender. Rhetorical inclusion of author and readers in evaluating evidence. No shared personal experience, actual knowledge, action or measured reader belief is claimed. The source’s intended readers are “invånarna i fiktiva Lervik”. |\\n| “Those” | Plural demonstrative referring to the 96 and 24 bookings. Source explicitly counts “bokningar, inte unika personer”. It asserts no number of distinct humans. |\\n| “they” in “why they choose” | Third person, plural, gender-neutral, referring to users asked during the proposed trial. Source has plural “användarna … varför de väljer telefon eller webb”. Both referent and grammatical number are supplied; no natural gender is inferred. |\\n| “It” in “It gives us a reason” | Third person, singular, nonhuman antecedent: telephone booking/using that route. Matches “Det är ett skäl …”. No natural-gender assertion. |\\n| “its” in “its value”, “it” in “the work it requires” | Third person, singular, nonhuman booking-route referent. Source discusses the retained telephone/web channels and weighing labour cost against user value. No human or gender assertion. |\\n| “its cost” in the two trial-cost passages | Third person, singular, nonhuman trial referent. Source explicitly gives “försöket” and “Kostnaden behöver kommunstyrelsen ta ställning till”. No claim that the cost is known. |\\n| “it” in “it can decide” | Third person, singular, nonhuman institutional referent: municipal executive board. Source “kommunstyrelsen … Därefter kan den avgöra …” supplies that referent and institutional singular. Swedish grammatical common gender in “den” does not become a human natural-gender claim. |\\n\\nOther demonstratives (“that route”, “That concern”, “that trial”, “After that”) refer respectively to telephone booking, the stated double-administration objection, the proposed six-month trial, and the preceding proposed sequence. Their referents are supplied; they carry no natural gender. “Both” and “each” refer to the two supplied channels, telephone and web. The draft introduces no he/she pronoun or other natural-gender assertion about any person. Sanna’s name alone has not been treated as gender evidence.\\n\\n## Findings\\n\\n### F1 — Source-support: measurement exclusion broadened across languages\\n\\nDraft: “The report measures neither age nor functional ability nor **familiarity with digital technology**.”\\n\\nSource: “Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller **digital vana**.”\\n\\nThe third term changes what is excluded from measurement. “Digital vana” concerns being accustomed to using digital technology. “Familiarity with digital technology” can also mean knowing about it without practical accustomedness. The source therefore does not establish every measurement absence the English sentence can assert.\\n\\n**Source-true/draft-false case:** the pilot report could include a test of users’ knowledge of digital tools learned from printed manuals, while measuring neither their practical accustomedness to using those tools, age, nor functional ability. It could still count bookings rather than unique people. Such a report would satisfy the supplied exclusions but would measure a form of familiarity. No supplied statement excludes that case; the source does not describe every other possible measure in the report.\\n\\n**Reverse-direction test:** a report measuring users’ habitual digital use while omitting a separate knowledge assessment could distinguish practical accustomedness from knowledge-based familiarity.
