{"session": "01a0f77d-5868-7a43-be28-5909f5150668", "timestamp": "2026-10-01T12:43:10.951Z", "call": {"type": "custom_tool_call", "id": "ctc_002f84ee0b6bb330016abe555ae47c87d2985ec508f3872e86", "status": "completed", "call_id": "call_ld7O5DVA76CDvdaeADK5tfPK", "name": "exec", "input": "const r = await tools.exec_command({cmd:\"cat source.md\",workdir:\"/private/var/folders/cs/vgp_x_353zd9frkjpysp7zdw0000gn/T/kntnt-native-session-r95dqkak/work\",max_output_tokens:20000}); text(r.output);\nconst d = await tools.exec_command({cmd:\"cat /private/var/folders/cs/vgp_x_353zd9frkjpysp7zdw0000gn/T/kntnt-native-session-r95dqkak/scratch/write-source-check-phwbhm4c/draft.md\",max_output_tokens:20000}); text(d.output);\n", "internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough": {"turn_id": "01a0f77d-5896-7a82-8405-2dbb4dd56daa", "create_time": 1790858584.187207}}}
{"session": "01a0f77d-5868-7a43-be28-5909f5150668", "timestamp": "2026-10-01T12:43:11.047Z", "result": {"type": "custom_tool_call_output", "id": "ctco_01a0f77d-7b47-7012-a227-72b5460ad6b2", "call_id": "call_ld7O5DVA76CDvdaeADK5tfPK", "output": [{"type": "input_text", "text": "Script completed\nWall time 0.1 seconds\nOutput:\n"}, {"type": "input_text", "text": "# Opinion brief and source\n\nSYNTHETIC EVALUATION MATERIAL. Fictional policy debate, not legal advice or a real municipal process. Source language: Swedish.\n\n## Brief\n\nSkriv en debattartikel för invånarna i fiktiva Lervik. Avsändare är Sanna Ek, talesperson för föreningen Öppna beslut. Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb. En tydlig, sakligt skarp text på ungefär 400 ord. Markdown med orienterande rubriker. Ingen kommersiell CTA.\n\n## Underlag\n\nKommunens pilotrapport Bokning av föreningslokaler, 8 april 2026, gäller ett åtta veckor långt försök i två lokaler. Under försöket gjordes 96 bokningar via webb och 24 via telefon. Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer. Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana. Telefonbokningarna kan därför inte användas för att säga hur stor andel av invånarna som inte kan boka digitalt.\n\nTjänsteutlåtandet inför kommunstyrelsens möte den 18 juni föreslår att telefonbokning tas bort för alla sju lokaler från september. Motivet i utlåtandet är att personalen ska slippa föra in uppgifter i två flöden. Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna. Förvaltningens verkliga invändning mot att behålla båda kanalerna är dubbel administration, inte att telefonanvändare är lata eller dyra.\n\nÖppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar. Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg och be användarna frivilligt ange varför de väljer telefon eller webb. Föreningen gör inget anspråk på att ha finansierat eller kostnadsberäknat försöket. Kostnaden behöver kommunstyrelsen ta ställning till. Därefter kan den avgöra om en kanal ska tas bort, ändras eller behållas.\n\nSanna Eks ståndpunkt: ”Att en bokning går via telefon är inte ett bevis för att någon inte kan använda webben. Det är ett skäl att ta reda på varför telefonen fortfarande används.” Detta får citeras eller refereras. Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna. Hon motsätter sig inte digital bokning.\n\n## Boundaries / judging\n\nInga lagkrav, protester, diskrimineringsfall, partipolitiska motiv eller besparingar är belagda. Siffror ska tillskrivas rapporten där de bär argumentet. Författaren ska kunna kritisera beslutsunderlaget skarpt utan falska motiv eller tandlös neutralisering. Slutet ska namnge kommunstyrelsens möjliga beslut, med kostnadsosäkerheten bevarad.\n"}, {"type": "input_text", "text": "# Lervik should test before ending telephone bookings\n\nBooking totals in Lervik’s pilot report do not measure residents’ ability to book online. The municipal executive board must weigh staff costs against the value of telephone access, yet the documents contain neither a time measurement nor a calculation of financial savings.\n\nBy Sanna Ek, spokesperson for Öppna beslut\n\nThe officer’s report for Lervik’s municipal executive board meeting on 18 June proposes removing telephone booking from all seven venues for local associations from September. I do not oppose digital booking. But on behalf of Öppna beslut, I urge the board to postpone a permanent switch and test telephone and web booking together for six months.\n\n## Bookings tell us too little about users\n\nThe municipality’s pilot report, dated 8 April 2026, records 96 web bookings and 24 telephone bookings during an eight-week trial at two venues. Those are bookings, not unique people. The report measures neither age nor functional ability nor familiarity with digital technology.\n\nSo the telephone figure cannot tell us what proportion of Lervik’s residents cannot book online. Nor does choosing the telephone prove an inability to use the web. It gives us a reason to ask why that route is still used.\n\n## Staff time needs a fair comparison\n\nThe administration’s objection to keeping both routes is the administrative work across two channels: staff enter information in two workflows. That concern deserves to be taken seriously. But the documents provide no measurement of the time involved or calculation of financial savings.\n\nRemoving a route before establishing its value to users and the work it requires leaves the board without the comparison it needs. Administrative simplicity is a reason to investigate the balance, not a substitute for doing so.\n\n## A wider trial can inform the choice\n\nÖppna beslut proposes keeping both booking routes for six months at all seven venues. During that trial, the administration should record the time spent on each route and ask users to say voluntarily why they choose telephone or web booking.\n\nWe make no claim to have financed or costed that trial. The executive board must consider its cost. Asking for better evidence does not remove the need to decide what gathering it is worth.\n\n## Evidence should guide the final booking decision\n\nLervik’s municipal executive board should now postpone the permanent switch and agree a six-month trial of both routes, with its cost explicitly considered. After that, it can decide whether to remove, change or retain a booking channel.\n"}], "internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough": {"turn_id": "01a0f77d-5896-7a82-8405-2dbb4dd56daa", "create_time": 1790858591.047536}}}
{"session": "01a0f77d-5868-7a43-be28-5909f5150668", "timestamp": "2026-10-01T12:49:15.752Z", "call": {"type": "custom_tool_call", "id": "ctc_002f84ee0b6bb330016abe55e7a22487d28b817e5fd1273c65", "status": "completed", "call_id": "call_KXtyzW9n7gRLFVJAxLEANBCK", "name": "exec", "input": "const report = String.raw`# Source and quotation comparison\n\n## Material examined and scope\n\nRead the complete supplied source, `source.md`, and the complete current draft, `draft.md`, including the headline, standfirst, byline and four subheadings. The source is Swedish; the draft is British English. The source identifies this as a fictional Lervik policy debate. There is no additional factual material and no direct speech quotation in the draft.\n\nThe comparison below distinguishes the author’s supported standpoint and reasoning from factual assertions. It does not require external evidence for an argument that the brief commissions Sanna Ek to make. It does require support for the factual premises of that argument, including the referents and restrictions introduced by translated terms.\n\n## Complete claim accounting\n\n### 1. Headline: requested action and timing\n\n**Draft:** “Lervik should test before ending telephone bookings”\n\n**Source:** “Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb.”\n\n**Comparison:** This is the commissioned recommendation, expressed as a headline. “Lervik” functions as the municipality whose executive board makes the decision; it does not assert an additional actor or a separate decision. “Before” preserves the order of trial first, permanent change later. The title does not assert that telephone booking has already ended. Its omitted trial length and permanent-change qualification are supplied in the article. Supported as the author’s advocacy.\n\n### 2. Standfirst: what booking totals establish\n\n**Draft:** “Booking totals in Lervik’s pilot report do not measure residents’ ability to book online.”\n\n**Source:** “Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer. Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana. Telefonbokningarna kan därför inte användas för att säga hur stor andel av invånarna som inte kan boka digitalt.”\n\n**Comparison:** The subject of the draft’s negative is the booking totals, not every conceivable item of information in the report. The source expressly distinguishes bookings from people and denies that telephone totals establish the share of residents unable to book digitally. The draft’s ordinary contextual meaning is that booking totals are no measurement of residents’ digital-booking ability. It does not assert that all residents can book online, that no caller has difficulty, or that the report contains a measured population prevalence. Supported.\n\n### 3. Standfirst: required comparison\n\n**Draft:** “The municipal executive board must weigh staff costs against the value of telephone access”\n\n**Source:** “Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna.”\n\n**Contextual source:** “Att en bokning går via telefon är inte ett bevis för att någon inte kan använda webben. Det är ett skäl att ta reda på varför telefonen fortfarande används.”\n\n**Comparison:** The source expressly attributes this position to Sanna. The byline and first-person body preserve her ownership of the argument; the standfirst reads as the article’s position, not as an independently established legal duty. “The municipal executive board” identifies the decision-making body named throughout the brief. “Staff costs” renders “arbetskostnaden”; “the value of telephone access” focuses the source’s value-to-users comparison on the route proposed for removal. That focus is the author’s supported argument about the proposal, rather than a claim that telephone access has a measured value. No legal requirement or quantified benefit is invented. Supported.\n\n### 4. Standfirst: absent measurements and calculations\n\n**Draft:** “yet the documents contain neither a time measurement nor a calculation of financial savings.”\n\n**Source:** “Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna.”\n\n**Comparison:** The subject, documentary scope and stated absences match. Financial savings is an idiomatic rendering of “ekonomisk besparingsberäkning” in this municipal staff-cost context. This says no calculation exists in the documents; it does not say savings cannot occur or have been disproved. Supported.\n\n### 5. Byline: author and role\n\n**Draft:** “By Sanna Ek, spokesperson for Öppna beslut”\n\n**Source:** “Avsändare är Sanna Ek, talesperson för föreningen Öppna beslut.”\n\n**Comparison:** The named author and spokesperson role match. The byline names the commissioned author, not the user. “Spokesperson” conveys no natural gender. The source later uses “Hon”, which conveys a female singular referent, but the draft adds no incompatible natural-gender assertion. Supported.\n\n### 6. Opening: identity and date of the administrative document\n\n**Draft:** “The officer’s report for Lervik’s municipal executive board meeting on 18 June”\n\n**Source:** “Tjänsteutlåtandet inför kommunstyrelsens möte den 18 juni”\n\n**Comparison:** The meeting, day and month match. No year has been added to the meeting date. “For” preserves the report’s relationship to the forthcoming meeting. “Municipal executive board” denotes “kommunstyrelsen” in this fictional municipality without importing UK council powers.\n\n“The officer’s report” introduces a singular officer as the report’s source or owner where “Tjänsteutlåtandet” identifies an administrative document without supplying the number or identity of its responsible officials. This provenance difference is finding SS-1 below.\n\n### 7. Opening: proposed removal, venues and start date\n\n**Draft:** “proposes removing telephone booking from all seven venues for local associations from September.”\n\n**Source:** “föreslår att telefonbokning tas bort för alla sju lokaler från september.”\n\n**Earlier source defining the premises:** “Kommunens pilotrapport Bokning av föreningslokaler, 8 april 2026, gäller ett åtta veckor långt försök i två lokaler.”\n\n**Brief defining the service:** “ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler”\n\n**Comparison:** Proposed status, telephone booking, all seven premises and September commencement match. The draft does not convert the proposal into an enacted decision. In this booking context “venues” identifies the premises, without asserting a particular building or room configuration. However, “local” modifies “associations” and adds a restriction absent from “föreningslokaler”. This is finding SS-2 below.\n\n### 8. Opening: stance on digital booking\n\n**Draft:** “I do not oppose digital booking.”\n\n**Source:** “Hon motsätter sig inte digital bokning.”\n\n**Comparison:** The first-person author is Sanna, established by the byline. The position matches. It does not assert support for every digital-only proposal. Supported.\n\n### 9. Opening: representation and six-month request\n\n**Draft:** “But on behalf of Öppna beslut, I urge the board to postpone a permanent switch and test telephone and web booking together for six months.”\n\n**Source:** “Avsändare är Sanna Ek, talesperson för föreningen Öppna beslut. Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb.”\n\n**Further source:** “Öppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar.”\n\n**Comparison:** The spokesperson’s representative capacity, postponement, permanent switch, both routes and duration match. The preceding removal proposal settles the referent of “a permanent switch”; it is the proposed move to online-only booking. This is advocacy, not a report that a trial has been approved or funded. Supported.\n\n### 10. First subheading: limits of booking information\n\n**Draft:** “Bookings tell us too little about users”\n\n**Source:** “Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer. Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana. Telefonbokningarna kan därför inte användas för att säga hur stor andel av invånarna som inte kan boka digitalt.”\n\n**Comparison:** “Too little” evaluates the totals against the decision and investigation the brief says Sanna advocates. It is not a numerical assessment that 24 bookings is high or low. The immediately following paragraph specifies the information limits. Supported as the author’s argument.\n\n### 11. Pilot identity, date, counts, duration and scope\n\n**Draft:** “The municipality’s pilot report, dated 8 April 2026, records 96 web bookings and 24 telephone bookings during an eight-week trial at two venues.”\n\n**Source:** “Kommunens pilotrapport Bokning av föreningslokaler, 8 april 2026, gäller ett åtta veckor långt försök i två lokaler. Under försöket gjordes 96 bokningar via webb och 24 via telefon.”\n\n**Comparison:** Municipality ownership, date, eight-week duration, two-premises scope, booking unit, channels and numbers match. The draft attributes both counts directly to the pilot report where they carry the argument, as the boundaries require. It does not turn counts into unique users, people or population shares. The document title is omitted without substituting a different named document. Supported.\n\n### 12. Unit counted\n\n**Draft:** “Those are bookings, not unique people.”\n\n**Source:** “Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer.”\n\n**Comparison:** “Those” refers to the 96 and 24 immediately preceding bookings. Both count and exclusion match. This does not state how many unique people booked. Supported.\n\n### 13. Variables not measured\n\n**Draft:** “The report measures neither age nor functional ability nor familiarity with digital technology.”\n\n**Source:** “Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana.”\n\n**Comparison:** The subject and all three excluded measurements match. In this user-access context, “functional ability” names the personal capacity denoted by “funktionsförmåga”, and “familiarity with digital technology” naturally denotes the practical digital familiarity denoted by “digital vana”. No particular diagnosis, disability prevalence or technical competence score is supplied. The wording does not mean merely knowledge of technology as an academic subject. No concrete contextual mismatch warrants a finding.\n\n### 14. Telephone count and population inference\n\n**Draft:** “So the telephone figure cannot tell us what proportion of Lervik’s residents cannot book online.”\n\n**Source:** “Telefonbokningarna kan därför inte användas för att säga hur stor andel av invånarna som inte kan boka digitalt.”\n\n**Comparison:** The antecedent is the reported count of 24 telephone bookings. Scope, population, channel and inability claim match. “Cannot tell us” preserves the source’s exclusion of that inference; it does not convert unknown inability into known ability. Supported.\n\n### 15. Telephone use does not prove inability\n\n**Draft:** “Nor does choosing the telephone prove an inability to use the web.”\n\n**Source:** “Sanna Eks ståndpunkt: ‘Att en bokning går via telefon är inte ett bevis för att någon inte kan använda webben.’”\n\n**Comparison:** This is an indirect rendering of Sanna’s explicitly supplied standpoint. Choosing telephone booking is not asserted to have any particular motive, and the sentence does not deny that an individual might be unable to use the web. “Choosing” fits the source’s later “varför de väljer telefon eller webb”; it does not assert unrestricted choice or the absence of barriers. Supported.\n\n### 16. Reason to investigate\n\n**Draft:** “It gives us a reason to ask why that route is still used.”\n\n**Source:** “Det är ett skäl att ta reda på varför telefonen fortfarande används.”\n\n**Comparison:** “It” is telephone booking/use, and “that route” is telephone booking. Ongoing use and the author’s reason to investigate match. No reason for using it is invented. Supported.\n\n### 17. Second subheading: comparison of staff time\n\n**Draft:** “Staff time needs a fair comparison”\n\n**Source:** “Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg”\n\n**Further source:** “Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna.”\n\n**Comparison:** The proposed channel-specific timing and Sanna’s comparison underpin this evaluative subheading. “Fair” is her advocacy about how to assess the proposal, not an attributed finding that an existing measured comparison is unfair. Supported.\n\n### 18. Administration’s objection and workflows\n\n**Draft:** “The administration’s objection to keeping both routes is the administrative work across two channels: staff enter information in two workflows.”\n\n**Source:** “Motivet i utlåtandet är att personalen ska slippa föra in uppgifter i två flöden. Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna. Förvaltningens verkliga invändning mot att behålla båda kanalerna är dubbel administration, inte att telefonanvändare är lata eller dyra.”\n\n**Comparison:** The draft preserves the actual supplied objection and its stated operational basis. The source’s desire that staff be spared entering information in two workflows supports the work being avoided. It does not introduce an amount of time or money, laziness, expense per telephone user, party motives or a causal savings estimate. “Across two channels” is specified by the following clause; it does not reduce the objection to mere existence of two access routes. Supported.\n\n### 19. Taking the concern seriously\n\n**Draft:** “That concern deserves to be taken seriously.”\n\n**Source:** “Förvaltningens verkliga invändning mot att behålla båda kanalerna är dubbel administration”\n\n**Further source:** “Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna.”\n\n**Comparison:** This is the author’s assessment of a supported objection. No outside party’s view is invented. It preserves the labour-cost side of the balance rather than changing the source’s factual motive. Supported argument.\n\n### 20. Documentary absence, repeated in body\n\n**Draft:** “But the documents provide no measurement of the time involved or calculation of financial savings.”\n\n**Source:** “Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna.”\n\n**Comparison:** The documentary scope and absent time/savings calculations match. The immediately preceding workflow detail settles “the time involved”. As in the standfirst, this is not a claim that nobody outside the documents has measured time or that savings are impossible. Consequence for the board’s comparison\n\n**Draft:** “Removing a route before establishing its value to users and the work it requires leaves the board without the comparison it needs.”\n\n**Source:** “Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna.”\n\n**Relevant factual source:** “Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna.”\n\n**Proposed investigation:** “Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg och be användarna frivilligt ange varför de väljer telefon eller webb.”\n\n**Comparison:** This is Sanna’s argument for gathering the missing channel-specific evidence before a decision. It is conditional reasoning about removal before establishing the relevant comparison, not a report of an actual removal, a quantified causal effect, or an assertion that no information about users exists anywhere. “A route” is generic within the two-route proposal; “its” and “it” refer to that route. The previous subheading and following “During that trial” preserve the proposal’s trial status; this is not an indefinitely funded service promise. Supported.\n\n### 25. Data gathering and voluntary reasons\n\n**Draft:** “During that trial, the administration should record the time spent on each route and ask users to say voluntarily why they choose telephone or web booking.”\n\n**Source:** “Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg och be användarna frivilligt ange varför de väljer telefon eller webb.”\n\n**Comparison:** The administration, proposed trial, per-route measurement, question, voluntary disclosure and two choices match. The draft’s “should” is a recommendation in the article and does not report an adopted administrative order. Voluntariness belongs to users giving reasons; the draft retains that scope. No age, disability or digital-literacy measurement is added. Supported.\n\n### 26. Financing and costing: no claim\n\n**Draft:** “We make no claim to have financed or costed that trial.”\n\n**Source:** “Föreningen gör inget anspråk på att ha finansierat eller kostnadsberäknat försöket.”\n\n**Comparison:** The preceding sentence identifies Öppna beslut, so “we” is the organisation speaking through its spokesperson. The draft retains the no-claim formulation. It does not replace it with an assertion that financing or costing definitely has not occurred. “Costed” is idiomatic British English for having calculated the trial’s costs. Supported.\n\n### 27. Trial cost and board’s responsibility\n\n**Draft:** “The executive board must consider its cost.”\n\n**Source:** “Kostnaden behöver kommunstyrelsen ta ställning till.”\n\n**Comparison:** The board and trial cost match. “Consider” alone is less explicit than taking a position, but the following sentence supplies the need to decide what the evidence-gathering is worth, and the final recommendation requires explicit cost consideration when agreeing the trial. Read in that context, the article does not claim a funded proposal or evade the board’s cost judgement. Supported; no factual certainty about the amount is introduced.\n\n### 28. Kostnaden behöver kommunstyrelsen ta ställning till.”\n\n**Comparison:** This is the author’s reflection on the supported cost uncertainty and board responsibility. “It” refers to evidence, not an unidentified person. Final subheading: evidence and decision\n\n**Draft:** “Evidence should guide the final booking decision”\n\n**Source:** “Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb.”\n\n**Further source:** “Därefter kan den avgöra om en kanal ska tas bort, ändras eller behållas.”\n\n**Comparison:** This is the commissioned evidence-first position. “Final” distinguishes the permanent channel decision from the preliminary trial; it does not claim the future decision is legally irrevocable. Supported advocacy.\n\n### 30. Final paragraph: immediate board recommendation and cost uncertainty\n\n**Draft:** “Lervik’s municipal executive board should now postpone the permanent switch and agree a six-month trial of both routes, with its cost explicitly considered.”\n\n**Source:** “Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb.”\n\n**Further source:** “Öppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar.”\n\n**Cost qualification:** “Föreningen gör inget anspråk på att ha finansierat eller kostnadsberäknat försöket. The draft does not prescribe retention as the only acceptable final decision. Supported.\n\n## Pronoun accounting\n\nPerson, number and natural gender are accounted for independently of merely locating each referent.\n\n- **“I”** in “I do not oppose” and “I urge”: first-person singular, no natural gender encoded. Swedish “Hon” is third-person singular and naturally female; changing to the authorised first-person authorial voice adds no gender claim.\n- **“Those”** in “Those are bookings”: plural demonstrative, no natural gender, referring to the reported bookings. These are inclusive author-and-reader positions in an opinion article for residents. They do not identify a measured group or assert that every resident holds Sanna’s opinion. The factual inference limits remain those supplied.\n- **“It”** in “It gives us a reason”: third-person singular, no natural gender, referring to the fact of telephone booking/use. Swedish “Det” similarly denotes that fact. Neither identifies a human whose gender would need support.\n- **“that route”**, **“both routes”**, **“each route”** and **“both booking routes”** refer to telephone and web booking. The source expressly supplies both channels and their uses. Singular “that route” is telephone; “each” distributes the proposed timing measurement over the two.\n- **“That concern”** denotes double administration. Demonstrative singular, no natural gender; the source expressly supplies the objection.\n- **“its”** in “its value to users” and **“it”** in “the work it requires”: third-person singular, no natural gender, referring to the hypothetical booking route. The source supplies routes and the cost/value comparison. The possessives do not assert a measured value or duration.\n- **“it”** in “the comparison it needs”: third-person singular, no natural gender, referring to the board. The source assigns the decision and needed comparison to the municipality/board. It does not assert that the board has publicly adopted Sanna’s view.\n- **“doing so”** refers to investigating the balance. It supplies no person, number or gender assertion.\n- **“they”** in “why they choose”: third-person plural, natural gender unspecified, referring to users. Source “användarna” and “de väljer” establish plural users; no female or male user population is asserted.\n- **“We”** in “We make no claim”: first-person plural, natural gender unspecified, the organisation’s institutional voice through its named spokesperson. The source’s singular “Föreningen” establishes who stands behind the statement; “we” does not assert a specific number of authors, staff or funded participants.\n- **“its”** in “consider its cost” and “with its cost explicitly considered”: third-person singular, no natural gender, referring to the trial. Source “Kostnaden” in the trial paragraph settles that relationship.\n- **“it”** in “gathering it”: third-person singular, no natural gender, referring to evidence. This is the author’s reasoning about the proposed data gathering.\n- **“that”** in “After that”: a singular demonstrative reference to the proposed trial and consideration sequence, with no natural gender.\n- **“it”** in “it can decide”: third-person singular, no natural gender, the board. Source “den” refers to “kommunstyrelsen”; Swedish common grammatical gender is not a separate natural-gender assertion about a human.\n\nThe draft’s nonpersonal English “it/its” references do not carry the source’s purely grammatical Swedish gender into human natural-gender claims. No masculine or female user group, unnamed individual user, first-hand personal experience or additional speaker has been invented.\n\n## Source-support findings and minimum repairs\n\n### SS-1. Singular officer provenance is not supplied\n\n**Draft passage:** “The officer’s report for Lervik’s municipal executive board meeting on 18 June”\n\n**Exact support:** “Tjänsteutlåtandet inför kommunstyrelsens möte den 18 juni”\n\n**Missing or changed support:** The source identifies the type and occasion of the administrative document. It supplies no singular officer, document owner or author count. The draft’s singular possessive adds that provenance.\n\n**Concrete two-direction test:**\n1. A municipal department could issue this tjänsteutlåtande jointly through several officials, with no single officer owning or authoring it. The supplied material permits this: it names the document and the administration but contains no statement excluding joint institutional authorship. The Swedish statement still holds; the singular “the officer’s report” does not have the provenance it names.\n2. Conversely, a single officer could produce a report for that meeting that is a supporting briefing rather than the formal tjänsteutlåtande. The draft’s generic document description could hold while the source’s specific administrative-document designation does not. The supplied material contains no statement equating every officer report with the tjänsteutlåtande.\n\n**Smallest supported repair:** Replace “The officer’s report” with “The administrative report”. Keep the meeting, proposal, dates and all substantive claims.\n\nThis is a source-support issue in translated descriptive prose, not a quotation-translation issue.\n\n### SS-2. “Local” restricts the associations beyond the source\n\n**Draft passage:** “all seven venues for local associations”\n\n**Exact support:** “alla sju lokaler”; their service is identified as “bokning av föreningslokaler”.\n\n**Missing or changed support:** “Föreningslokaler” supplies premises for associations. It does not say those associations must be locally based. The brief’s local audience and fictional municipal setting do not establish the associations’ geographical scope.\n\n**Concrete two-direction test:**\n1. A nationwide association could use one of the municipality’s association premises. “Föreningslokaler” remains true, while classifying those premises as being for “local associations” adds a restriction that need not hold. Nothing supplied excludes national associations or their bookings.\n2. If a venue is for local associations, it is also a venue for associations; there is no reverse counterexample on that restriction. The asymmetry confirms that the draft narrows the source term rather than reproducing its scope.\n\n**Smallest supported repair:** Delete “local”, producing “all seven venues for associations”.\n\nThis is a source-support issue in translated descriptive prose, not a quotation-translation issue.\n\n## Quotation comparison and other boundaries\n\nThere are no direct speech quotations in the draft, so there is no translated-quotation voice or idiom finding. The indirect rendering of Sanna’s supplied quotation is supported and retains her position, its negation and its reason to investigate.\n\nNo unsupported legal duty, protest, discrimination event, party motive, savings amount, causal saving effect, telephone-user motive or personal experience appears. Reported figures are attributed where they support the argument. Cost uncertainty and the board’s three possible final decisions remain explicit. Editorial judgments about the comparison and the trial belong to the commissioned author and are defensible as written.\n\n## Completion status\n\nComparison complete. {"session": "01a0f77d-5868-7a43-be28-5909f5150668", "timestamp": "2026-10-01T12:53:00.392Z", "call": {"type": "custom_tool_call", "id": "ctc_002f84ee0b6bb330016abe56f8535487d2999fa3cee5c6eafa", "status": "completed", "call_id": "call_0y9VDmysECK6xam4xeWXTfM4", "name": "exec", "input": "const report = String.raw`# Source and quotation comparison\n\n## Material examined and scope\n\nRead the complete supplied source, source.md, and the complete current draft, draft.md, including the headline, standfirst, byline and four subheadings. The source is Swedish; the draft is British English. The source identifies this as a fictional Lervik policy debate. There is no additional factual material and no direct speech quotation in the draft.\n\nThe comparison below distinguishes the author’s supported standpoint and reasoning from factual assertions. It does not require external evidence for an argument that the brief commissions Sanna Ek to make. It does require support for the factual premises of that argument, including the referents and restrictions introduced by translated terms.\n\n## Complete claim accounting\n\n### 1. Headline: requested action and timing\n\n**Draft:** “Lervik should test before ending telephone bookings”\n\n**Source:** “Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb.”\n\n**Comparison:** This is the commissioned recommendation, expressed as a headline. “Lervik” functions as the municipality whose executive board makes the decision; it does not assert an additional actor or a separate decision. “Before” preserves the order of trial first, permanent change later. The title does not assert that telephone booking has already ended. Its omitted trial length and permanent-change qualification are supplied in the article. Supported as the author’s advocacy.\n\n### 2. Standfirst: what booking totals establish\n\n**Draft:** “Booking totals in Lervik’s pilot report do not measure residents’ ability to book online.”\n\n**Source:** “Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer. Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana. Telefonbokningarna kan därför inte användas för att säga hur stor andel av invånarna som inte kan boka digitalt.”\n\n**Comparison:** The subject of the draft’s negative is the booking totals, not every conceivable item of information in the report. The source expressly distinguishes bookings from people and denies that telephone totals establish the share of residents unable to book digitally. The draft’s ordinary contextual meaning is that booking totals are no measurement of residents’ digital-booking ability. It does not assert that all residents can book online, that no caller has difficulty, or that the report contains a measured population prevalence. Supported.\n\n### 3. Standfirst: required comparison\n\n**Draft:** “The municipal executive board must weigh staff costs against the value of telephone access”\n\n**Source:** “Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna.”\n\n**Contextual source:** “Att en bokning går via telefon är inte ett bevis för att någon inte kan använda webben. Det är ett skäl att ta reda på varför telefonen fortfarande används.”\n\n**Comparison:** The source expressly attributes this position to Sanna. The byline and first-person body preserve her ownership of the argument; the standfirst reads as the article’s position, not as an independently established legal duty. “The municipal executive board” identifies the decision-making body named throughout the brief. “Staff costs” renders “arbetskostnaden”; “the value of telephone access” focuses the source’s value-to-users comparison on the route proposed for removal. That focus is the author’s supported argument about the proposal, rather than a claim that telephone access has a measured value. No legal requirement or quantified benefit is invented. Supported.\n\n### 4. Standfirst: absent measurements and calculations\n\n**Draft:** “yet the documents contain neither a time measurement nor a calculation of financial savings.”\n\n**Source:** “Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna.”\n\n**Comparison:** The subject, documentary scope and stated absences match. Financial savings is an idiomatic rendering of “ekonomisk besparingsberäkning” in this municipal staff-cost context. This says no calculation exists in the documents; it does not say savings cannot occur or have been disproved. Supported.\n\n### 5. Byline: author and role\n\n**Draft:** “By Sanna Ek, spokesperson for Öppna beslut”\n\n**Source:** “Avsändare är Sanna Ek, talesperson för föreningen Öppna beslut.”\n\n**Comparison:** The named author and spokesperson role match. The byline names the commissioned author, not the user. “Spokesperson” conveys no natural gender. The source later uses “Hon”, which conveys a female singular referent, but the draft adds no incompatible natural-gender assertion. Supported.\n\n### 6. Opening: identity and date of the administrative document\n\n**Draft:** “The officer’s report for Lervik’s municipal executive board meeting on 18 June”\n\n**Source:** “Tjänsteutlåtandet inför kommunstyrelsens möte den 18 juni”\n\n**Comparison:** The meeting, day and month match. No year has been added to the meeting date. “For” preserves the report’s relationship to the forthcoming meeting. “Municipal executive board” denotes “kommunstyrelsen” in this fictional municipality without importing UK council powers.\n\n“The officer’s report” introduces a singular officer as the report’s source or owner where “Tjänsteutlåtandet” identifies an administrative document without supplying the number or identity of its responsible officials. This provenance difference is finding SS-1 below.\n\n### 7. Opening: proposed removal, venues and start date\n\n**Draft:** “proposes removing telephone booking from all seven venues for local associations from September.”\n\n**Source:** “föreslår att telefonbokning tas bort för alla sju lokaler från september.”\n\n**Earlier source defining the premises:** “Kommunens pilotrapport Bokning av föreningslokaler, 8 april 2026, gäller ett åtta veckor långt försök i två lokaler.”\n\n**Brief defining the service:** “ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler”\n\n**Comparison:** Proposed status, telephone booking, all seven premises and September commencement match. The draft does not convert the proposal into an enacted decision. In this booking context “venues” identifies the premises, without asserting a particular building or room configuration. However, “local” modifies “associations” and adds a restriction absent from “föreningslokaler”. This is finding SS-2 below.\n\n### 8. Opening: stance on digital booking\n\n**Draft:** “I do not oppose digital booking.”\n\n**Source:** “Hon motsätter sig inte digital bokning.”\n\n**Comparison:** The first-person author is Sanna, established by the byline. The position matches. It does not assert support for every digital-only proposal. Supported.\n\n### 9. Opening: representation and six-month request\n\n**Draft:** “But on behalf of Öppna beslut, I urge the board to postpone a permanent switch and test telephone and web booking together for six months.”\n\n**Source:** “Avsändare är Sanna Ek, talesperson för föreningen Öppna beslut. Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb.”\n\n**Further source:** “Öppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar.”\n\n**Comparison:** The spokesperson’s representative capacity, postponement, permanent switch, both routes and duration match. The preceding removal proposal settles the referent of “a permanent switch”; it is the proposed move to online-only booking. This is advocacy, not a report that a trial has been approved or funded. Supported.\n\n### 10. First subheading: limits of booking information\n\n**Draft:** “Bookings tell us too little about users”\n\n**Source:** “Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer. Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana. Telefonbokningarna kan därför inte användas för att säga hur stor andel av invånarna som inte kan boka digitalt.”\n\n**Comparison:** “Too little” evaluates the totals against the decision and investigation the brief says Sanna advocates. It is not a numerical assessment that 24 bookings is high or low. The immediately following paragraph specifies the information limits. Supported as the author’s argument.\n\n### 11. Pilot identity, date, counts, duration and scope\n\n**Draft:** “The municipality’s pilot report, dated 8 April 2026, records 96 web bookings and 24 telephone bookings during an eight-week trial at two venues.”\n\n**Source:** “Kommunens pilotrapport Bokning av föreningslokaler, 8 april 2026, gäller ett åtta veckor långt försök i två lokaler. Under försöket gjordes 96 bokningar via webb och 24 via telefon.”\n\n**Comparison:** Municipality ownership, date, eight-week duration, two-premises scope, booking unit, channels and numbers match. The draft attributes both counts directly to the pilot report where they carry the argument, as the boundaries require. It does not turn counts into unique users, people or population shares. The document title is omitted without substituting a different named document. Supported.\n\n### 12. Unit counted\n\n**Draft:** “Those are bookings, not unique people.”\n\n**Source:** “Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer.”\n\n**Comparison:** “Those” refers to the 96 and 24 immediately preceding bookings. Both count and exclusion match. This does not state how many unique people booked. Supported.\n\n### 13. Variables not measured\n\n**Draft:** “The report measures neither age nor functional ability nor familiarity with digital technology.”\n\n**Source:** “Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana.”\n\n**Comparison:** The subject and all three excluded measurements match. In this user-access context, “functional ability” names the personal capacity denoted by “funktionsförmåga”, and “familiarity with digital technology” naturally denotes the practical digital familiarity denoted by “digital vana”. No particular diagnosis, disability prevalence or technical competence score is supplied. The wording does not mean merely knowledge of technology as an academic subject. No concrete contextual mismatch warrants a finding.\n\n### 14. Telephone count and population inference\n\n**Draft:** “So the telephone figure cannot tell us what proportion of Lervik’s residents cannot book online.”\n\n**Source:** “Telefonbokningarna kan därför inte användas för att säga hur stor andel av invånarna som inte kan boka digitalt.”\n\n**Comparison:** The antecedent is the reported count of 24 telephone bookings. Scope, population, channel and inability claim match. “Cannot tell us” preserves the source’s exclusion of that inference; it does not convert unknown inability into known ability. Supported.\n\n### 15. Telephone use does not prove inability\n\n**Draft:** “Nor does choosing the telephone prove an inability to use the web.”\n\n**Source:** “Sanna Eks ståndpunkt: ‘Att en bokning går via telefon är inte ett bevis för att någon inte kan använda webben.’”\n\n**Comparison:** This is an indirect rendering of Sanna’s explicitly supplied standpoint. Choosing telephone booking is not asserted to have any particular motive, and the sentence does not deny that an individual might be unable to use the web. “Choosing” fits the source’s later “varför de väljer telefon eller webb”; it does not assert unrestricted choice or the absence of barriers. Supported.\n\n### 16. Reason to investigate\n\n**Draft:** “It gives us a reason to ask why that route is still used.”\n\n**Source:** “Det är ett skäl att ta reda på varför telefonen fortfarande används.”\n\n**Comparison:** “It” is telephone booking/use, and “that route” is telephone booking. Ongoing use and the author’s reason to investigate match. No reason for using it is invented. Supported.\n\n### 17. Second subheading: comparison of staff time\n\n**Draft:** “Staff time needs a fair comparison”\n\n**Source:** “Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg”\n\n**Further source:** “Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna.”\n\n**Comparison:** The proposed channel-specific timing and Sanna’s comparison underpin this evaluative subheading. “Fair” is her advocacy about how to assess the proposal, not an attributed finding that an existing measured comparison is unfair. It does not introduce an amount of time or money, laziness, expense per telephone user, party motives or a causal savings estimate. “Across two channels” is specified by the following clause; it does not reduce the objection to mere existence of two access routes. Documentary absence, repeated in body\n\n**Draft:** “But the documents provide no measurement of the time involved or calculation of financial savings.”\n\n**Source:** “Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna.”\n\n**Comparison:** The documentary scope and absent time/savings calculations match. The draft does not prescribe retention as the only acceptable final decision. Supported.\n\n## Pronoun accounting\n\nPerson, number and natural gender are accounted for independently of merely locating each referent.\n\n- **“I”** in “I do not oppose” and “I urge”: first-person singular, no natural gender encoded. The source supplies Sanna as the author/spokesperson, gives her non-opposition, and commissions the recommendation. Swedish “Hon” is third-person singular and naturally female; changing to the authorised first-person authorial voice adds no gender claim.\n- **“Those”** in “Those are bookings”: plural demonstrative, no natural gender, referring to the reported bookings. Source “bokningar” and the two counts establish plurality.\n- **“us”** in “tell us too little”, “cannot tell us” and “gives us a reason”: first-person plural, no natural gender. These are inclusive author-and-reader positions in an opinion article for residents. They do not identify a measured group or assert that every resident holds Sanna’s opinion. The factual inference limits remain those supplied.\n- **“It”** in “It gives us a reason”: third-person singular, no natural gender, referring to the fact of telephone booking/use. Source “användarna” and “de väljer” establish plural users; no female or male user population is asserted.\n- **“We”** in “We make no claim”: first-person plural, natural gender unspecified, the organisation’s institutional voice through its named spokesperson. The source’s singular “Föreningen” establishes who stands behind the statement; “we” does not assert a specific number of authors, staff or funded participants.\n- **“its”** in “consider its cost” and “with its cost explicitly considered”: third-person singular, no natural gender, referring to the trial. Source “Kostnaden” in the trial paragraph settles that relationship.\n- **“it”** in “gathering it”: third-person singular, no natural gender, referring to evidence. This is the author’s reasoning about the proposed data gathering.\n- **“that”** in “After that”: a singular demonstrative reference to the proposed trial and consideration sequence, with no natural gender.\n- **“it”** in “it can decide”: third-person singular, no natural gender, the board. Source “den” refers to “kommunstyrelsen”; Swedish common grammatical gender is not a separate natural-gender assertion about a human.\n\nThe draft’s nonpersonal English it/its references do not carry the source’s purely grammatical Swedish gender into human natural-gender claims. No masculine or female user group, unnamed individual user, first-hand personal experience or additional speaker has been invented.\n\n## Source-support findings and minimum repairs\n\n### SS-1. Singular officer provenance is not supplied\n\n**Draft passage:** “The officer’s report for Lervik’s municipal executive board meeting on 18 June”\n\n**Exact support:** “Tjänsteutlåtandet inför kommunstyrelsens möte den 18 juni”\n\n**Missing or changed support:** The source identifies the type and occasion of the administrative document. It supplies no singular officer, document owner or author count. The draft’s singular possessive adds that provenance.\n\n**Concrete two-direction test:**\n1. A municipal department could issue this tjänsteutlåtande jointly through several officials, with no single officer owning or authoring it. The supplied material permits this: it names the document and the administration but contains no statement excluding joint institutional authorship. The Swedish statement still holds; the singular “the officer’s report” does not have the provenance it names.\n2. Conversely, a single officer could produce a report for that meeting that is a supporting briefing rather than the formal tjänsteutlåtande. The draft’s generic document description could hold while the source’s specific administrative-document designation does not. The supplied material contains no statement equating every officer report with the tjänsteutlåtande.\n\n**Smallest supported repair:** Replace “The officer’s report” with “The administrative report”. Keep the meeting, proposal, dates and all substantive claims.\n\nThis is a source-support issue in translated descriptive prose, not a quotation-translation issue.\n\n### SS-2. “Local” restricts the associations beyond the source\n\n**Draft passage:** “all seven venues for local associations”\n\n**Exact support:** “alla sju lokaler”; their service is identified as “bokning av föreningslokaler”.\n\n**Missing or changed support:** “Föreningslokaler” supplies premises for associations. It does not say those associations must be locally based. The brief’s local audience and fictional municipal setting do not establish the associations’ geographical scope.\n\n**Concrete two-direction test:**\n1. A nationwide association could use one of the municipality’s association premises. “Föreningslokaler” remains true, while classifying those premises as being for “local associations” adds a restriction that need not hold. Nothing supplied excludes national associations or their bookings.\n2. If a venue is for local associations, it is also a venue for associations; there is no reverse counterexample on that restriction. The asymmetry confirms that the draft narrows the source term rather than reproducing its scope.\n\n**Smallest supported repair:** Delete “local”, producing “all seven venues for associations”.\n\nThis is a source-support issue in translated descriptive prose, not a quotation-translation issue.\n\n## Quotation comparison and other boundaries\n\nThere are no direct speech quotations in the draft, so there is no translated-quotation voice or idiom finding. The indirect rendering of Sanna’s supplied quotation is supported and retains her position, its negation and its reason to investigate.\n\nNo unsupported legal duty, protest, discrimination event, party motive, savings amount, causal saving effect, telephone-user motive or personal experience appears. Reported figures are attributed where they support the argument. Cost uncertainty and the board’s three possible final decisions remain explicit. Editorial judgments about the comparison and the trial belong to the commissioned author and are defensible as written.\n\n## Completion status\n\nComparison complete. No sources or draft were changed.\n\n## Claim accounting\n\nFor each item below, quoted source wording retains the qualifications needed for the comparison. Authorial arguments are identified separately from claims about facts.\n\n1. **Headline: “Lervik should test before ending telephone bookings”.**\n   Source: “Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb.”\n   This is the commissioned recommendation. The municipal place name stands for its decision-making body. “Before” retains the order; the body supplies the trial length and permanent-change qualification. It does not say telephone booking has already ended. Supported advocacy.\n\n2. **Standfirst: “Booking totals in Lervik’s pilot report do not measure residents’ ability to book online.”**\n   Source: “Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer. It does not assert that residents can book online or that callers have no difficulties. Supported explanation of the count’s limits.\n\n3. **Standfirst: “The municipal executive board must weigh staff costs against the value of telephone access”.**\n   Source: “Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna.”\n   Context: “Att en bokning går via telefon är inte ett bevis för att någon inte kan använda webben. Det är ett skäl att ta reda på varför telefonen fortfarande används.”\n   The byline and first-person body preserve Sanna’s ownership of this position. This is advocacy, not an asserted legal duty. Focusing user value on the route proposed for removal is supported argument about that proposal, not an invented measured benefit. Staff costs renders “arbetskostnaden”; the board is the decision-maker identified in the brief.\n\n4. **Standfirst: “yet the documents contain neither a time measurement nor a calculation of financial savings.”**\n   Source: “Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna.”\n   The documentary scope and both absences match. “Financial savings” is idiomatic for the supplied economic savings calculation in this staff-cost context. No claim that savings are impossible is added.\n\n5. **Byline: “By Sanna Ek, spokesperson for Öppna beslut”.**\n   Source: “Avsändare är Sanna Ek, talesperson för föreningen Öppna beslut.”\n   Author and role match; the byline does not identify the user. English “spokesperson” asserts no natural gender.\n\n6. **Opening: “The officer’s report for Lervik’s municipal executive board meeting on 18 June”.**\n   Source: “Tjänsteutlåtandet inför kommunstyrelsens möte den 18 juni”\n   Body, meeting and date match; no meeting year has been introduced. “For” retains the report’s connection with the forthcoming meeting. “The officer’s” adds singular officer provenance not supplied by the document designation. See SS-1.\n\n7. **Opening: “proposes removing telephone booking from all seven venues for local associations from September.”**\n   Source: “föreslår att telefonbokning tas bort för alla sju lokaler från september.”\n   Defining context: “ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler”; “Kommunens pilotrapport Bokning av föreningslokaler”.\n   Proposed status, telephone route, all seven premises and September match. No adopted decision is invented. “Venues” is compatible with the bookable premises in this context. “Local associations” adds a geographical restriction to the associations. See SS-2.\n\n8. **Opening: “I do not oppose digital booking.”**\n   Source: “Hon motsätter sig inte digital bokning.”\n   The authorised first-person author is Sanna. The stance matches and does not imply support for every online-only proposal.\n\n9. **Opening: “But on behalf of Öppna beslut, I urge the board to postpone a permanent switch and test telephone and web booking together for six months.”**\n   Source: “Avsändare är Sanna Ek, talesperson för föreningen Öppna beslut. Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb.”\n   Further source: “Öppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar.”\n   Representative capacity, requested postponement, permanence, both routes and duration match. The previous sentence identifies the switch. This does not report approval or financing.\n\n10. **Subheading: “Bookings tell us too little about users”.**\n    Source: “Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer. Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana. Telefonbokningarna kan därför inte användas för att säga hur stor andel av invånarna som inte kan boka digitalt.”\n    “Too little” is the author’s evaluation against the information needed for this decision. It does not characterise the count of 24 as high or low. The following prose specifies the information limits. Supported argument.\n\n11. **Pilot paragraph: “The municipality’s pilot report, dated 8 April 2026, records 96 web bookings and 24 telephone bookings during an eight-week trial at two venues.”**\n    Source: “Kommunens pilotrapport Bokning av föreningslokaler, 8 april 2026, gäller ett åtta veckor långt försök i två lokaler. Under försöket gjordes 96 bokningar via webb och 24 via telefon.”\n    Report attribution, date, counts, booking unit, channels, eight weeks and two premises match. The figures are explicitly attributed where they support the argument. Omission of the report’s title does not introduce a different title.\n\n12. **“Those are bookings, not unique people.”**\n    Source: “Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer.”\n    Same unit and exclusion; unique people are not counted or estimated.\n\n13. **“The report measures neither age nor functional ability nor familiarity with digital technology.”**\n    Source: “Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana.”\n    All three exclusions match. In this user-access context, “functional ability” denotes personal functional capacity, and “familiarity with digital technology” denotes practical digital familiarity. The latter is not read here as academic knowledge about technology. No concrete contextual obstruction, diagnosis or population figure has been added.\n\n14. **“So the telephone figure cannot tell us what proportion of Lervik’s residents cannot book online.”**\n    Source: “Telefonbokningarna kan därför inte användas för att säga hur stor andel av invånarna som inte kan boka digitalt.”\n    Population scope, inference limit, inability and channel match. Unknown inability has not been converted into proven ability.\n\n15. **“Nor does choosing the telephone prove an inability to use the web.”**\n    Source: “Sanna Eks ståndpunkt: ‘Att en bokning går via telefon är inte ett bevis för att någon inte kan använda webben.’”\n    The stance is Sanna’s, indirectly rendered without quotation marks. “Choosing” also has support in “varför de väljer telefon eller webb”; it does not assert unconstrained choices or no barriers. Continued use matches; no reason for that use is asserted as known.\n\n17. **Subheading: “Staff time needs a fair comparison”.**\n    Source: “Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg”; “Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna.”\n    Supported authorial evaluation of the proposed comparison, without asserting an existing measured comparison or quantified unfairness.\n\n18. **“The administration’s objection to keeping both routes is the administrative work across two channels: staff enter information in two workflows.”**\n    Source: “Motivet i utlåtandet är att personalen ska slippa föra in uppgifter i två flöden. Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna. Förvaltningens verkliga invändning mot att behålla båda kanalerna är dubbel administration, inte att telefonanvändare är lata eller dyra.”\n    Actual supplied objection and operational explanation match. Avoiding entry in two workflows supports the work being avoided. There is no time amount, cost amount, laziness, party motive or causal savings claim.\n\n19. **“That concern deserves to be taken seriously.”**\n    Source: “Förvaltningens verkliga invändning mot att behålla båda kanalerna är dubbel administration”; “Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna.”\n    The author assesses a supported concern; no view is attributed to a new person. Supported argument.\n\n20. **“But the documents provide no measurement of the time involved or calculation of financial savings.”**\n    Source: “Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna.”\n    Same documentary scope and absences. The preceding workflow clause settles “time involved”. No absence outside the documents is claimed.\n\n21. **“Removing a route before establishing its value to users and the work it requires leaves the board without the comparison it needs.”**\n    Source: “Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna.”\n    Factual premise: “Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna.”\n    Proposed investigation: “Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg och be användarna frivilligt ange varför de väljer telefon eller webb.”\n    This is conditional authorial reasoning about removal before the relevant comparison. It does not report an actual removal, a quantified causal effect or the absence of every form of user information everywhere.\n\n22. **“Administrative simplicity is a reason to investigate the balance, not a substitute for doing so.”**\n    Source: “Motivet i utlåtandet är att personalen ska slippa föra in uppgifter i två flöden”; “Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna.”\n    Characterising avoidance of double administration as an aim of simplicity is supported. The remaining clause is Sanna’s argument, not a measured technical effect or consensus.\n\n23. **Subheading: “A wider trial can inform the choice”.**\n    Source: “ett åtta veckor långt försök i två lokaler”; “Öppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar. Subheading and next sentence retain trial status.\n\n25. **“During that trial, the administration should record the time spent on each route and ask users to say voluntarily why they choose telephone or web booking.”**\n    Source: “Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg och be användarna frivilligt ange varför de väljer telefon eller webb.”\n    Actor, proposed trial, measurement per route, voluntary user disclosure and options match. “Should” is recommendation, not a report of an adopted order. No additional user variables are introduced.\n\n26. **“We make no claim to have financed or costed that trial.”**\n    Source: “Föreningen gör inget anspråk på att ha finansierat eller kostnadsberäknat försöket.”\n    The institutional speaker is the preceding Öppna beslut. No claim of financing or costing remains distinct from knowing that neither happened. British “costed” means having calculated costs. Supported.\n\n27. **“The executive board must consider its cost.”**\n    Source: “Kostnaden behöver kommunstyrelsen ta ställning till.”\n    Body and trial cost match. “Consider” is less explicit in isolation than taking a position, but the next sentence supplies deciding what evidence-gathering is worth and the final recommendation puts cost into the board’s trial decision. In context the cost judgement and uncertainty are preserved.\n\n28. **“Asking for better evidence does not remove the need to decide what gathering it is worth.”**\n    Source: “Föreningen gör inget anspråk på att ha finansierat eller kostnadsberäknat försöket. No amount, available budget or affordability conclusion is given.\n\n29. **Subheading: “Evidence should guide the final booking decision”.**\n    Source: “Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb”; “Därefter kan den avgöra om en kanal ska tas bort, ändras eller behållas.”\n    Supported commissioned position. “Final” distinguishes permanent channel choice from trial; it does not assert legal irreversibility.\n\n30. **“Lervik’s municipal executive board should now postpone the permanent switch and agree a six-month trial of both routes, with its cost explicitly considered.”**\n    Source: “Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb”; “Öppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar”; “Föreningen gör inget anspråk på att ha finansierat eller kostnadsberäknat försöket. Kostnaden behöver kommunstyrelsen ta ställning till.”\n    Actor, recommendation, permanence, period and two routes match. “Now” is advocated timing, not an event. “Agree” is requested, not reported approval. Explicit cost consideration plus the preceding no-claim statement preserves uncertainty.\n\n31. **“After that, it can decide whether to remove, change or retain a booking channel.”**\n    Source: “Kostnaden behöver kommunstyrelsen ta ställning till. Därefter kan den avgöra om en kanal ska tas bort, ändras eller behållas.”\n    The board, post-trial and cost-consideration sequence, modality and three possible decisions match. Retention is not presented as the only acceptable result.\n\n## Separate pronoun accounting\n\n- “I” in the two opening uses: first-person singular, natural gender unspecified. Authorship and the position are supplied. Source “Hon” is third-person singular and naturally female; the authorised authorial change adds no gender claim.\n- “Those” in “Those are bookings”: plural demonstrative, natural gender unspecified; the supplied counts and “bokningar” establish plurality.\n- “us” in the first subheading, population-inference sentence and investigation sentence: first-person plural, natural gender unspecified; inclusive author/reader rhetoric for an article to residents. It neither counts a population nor attributes Sanna’s view to every resident.\n- “It” in “It gives us a reason”: third-person singular, natural gender unspecified, a reference to telephone use as a fact. Swedish “Det” likewise denotes that fact.\n- “that route”, “both routes”, “each route” and “both booking routes”: singular or distributed/plural as stated, natural gender unspecified; the source expressly supplies telephone and web routes.\n- “That concern”: singular demonstrative, natural gender unspecified; the supplied double-administration objection.\n- “its” in “its value to users” and “it” in “the work it requires”: third-person singular, natural gender unspecified; a hypothetical route in authorial reasoning. They add no measured value or work amount.\n- “it” in “the comparison it needs”: third-person singular, natural gender unspecified; the board in Sanna’s argument. It does not assert that the board itself has publicly adopted her assessment.\n- “doing so”: refers to investigating the balance; no person, number or natural-gender claim.\n- “they” in “why they choose”: third-person plural, natural gender unspecified; source “användarna” and “de väljer” establish plural users without male/female composition.\n- “We” in “We make no claim”: first-person plural, natural gender unspecified; organisational voice through its spokesperson. Source “Föreningen” supplies the asserting party, not a specific count of authors, staff or participants.\n- “its” in the two trial-cost passages: third-person singular, natural gender unspecified; source “Kostnaden” in the trial paragraph supplies the trial-cost relationship.\n- “it” in “gathering it”: third-person singular, natural gender unspecified; evidence in the author’s cost reflection.\n- “that” in “After that”: singular demonstrative, natural gender unspecified; the proposed trial/consideration sequence.\n- Final “it can decide”: third-person singular, natural gender unspecified; source “den” refers to “kommunstyrelsen”. Swedish grammatical common gender does not assert a human’s natural gender.\n\nNo additional human gender, personal experience, individual user or speaker is introduced through these pronouns.\n\n## Source-support findings\n\n### SS-1: singular officer provenance\n\nDraft: “The officer’s report for Lervik’s municipal executive board meeting on 18 June”.\n\nExact source: “Tjänsteutlåtandet inför kommunstyrelsens möte den 18 juni”.\n\nThe source names an administrative document, without a singular officer or its authorship. The draft’s singular possessive adds that provenance.\n\nTwo-direction test:\n- A municipal department could issue the tjänsteutlåtande jointly through several officials, with no single officer owning or authoring it. The supplied material names the administration and document, but contains no statement excluding that case. The source’s designation holds; the singular officer provenance does not.\n- Conversely, a single officer could produce a supporting report for the meeting that is not the formal tjänsteutlåtande. The draft’s generic description could hold while the specific source-document designation does not. No supplied statement equates every officer report with that formal document.\n\nSmallest supported repair: replace “The officer’s report” with “The administrative report”. Preserve its meeting, dates, proposal and other substantive details.\n\n### SS-2: geographical restriction on associations\n\nDraft: “all seven venues for local associations”.\n\nExact source: “alla sju lokaler”; the premises are defined by “bokning av föreningslokaler”.\n\nThe source does not restrict associations to being locally based. No supplied statement excludes national associations or their bookings.\n- Every venue for local associations is also a venue for associations; there is no reverse counterexample on this restriction. The asymmetry shows that the draft narrows the source.\n\nSmallest supported repair: delete “local”: “all seven venues for associations”.\n\nBoth findings concern source support in translated descriptive prose, rather than translation of quoted speech.\n\n## Quotation and boundary results\n\nThere are no direct speech quotations, so no quotation-translation idiom or distinctive-voice findings. Sanna’s indirectly rendered supplied statement preserves its meaning and attribution.\n\nNo unsupported legal requirement, protest, discrimination event, party motive, savings amount, causal saving effect, telephone-user motive or personal experience appears. Trial cost uncertainty and the board’s three possible final channel decisions remain explicit. The author’s rhetorical evaluations and recommendations are supported as her position rather than externally proved facts.\n\n## Completion status\n\nComparison complete. Source and draft unchanged. {"session": "01a0f78b-98a3-7e41-9c67-c1340c68f96e", "timestamp": "2026-10-01T12:58:48.343Z", "result": {"type": "custom_tool_call_output", "id": "ctco_01a0f78b-c897-70d2-a3f8-8f682c0b64d3", "call_id": "call_zuzE45n29C8zNuWh7Jjp773T", "output": [{"type": "input_text", "text": "Script completed\nWall time 0.0 seconds\nOutput:\n"}, {"type": "input_text", "text": "{\"file\":\"source.md\",\"result\":{\"status\":\"fulfilled\",\"value\":{\"chunk_id\":\"808cfc\",\"wall_time_seconds\":5.83e-7,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":673,\"output\":\"# Opinion brief and source\\n\\nSYNTHETIC EVALUATION MATERIAL. Fictional policy debate, not legal advice or a real municipal process. Source language: Swedish.\\n\\n## Brief\\n\\nSkriv en debattartikel för invånarna i fiktiva Lervik. Avsändare är Sanna Ek, talesperson för föreningen Öppna beslut. Tes: kommunstyrelsen bör skjuta upp ett permanent byte till enbart digital bokning av föreningslokaler och först pröva ett halvår med både telefon och webb. En tydlig, sakligt skarp text på ungefär 400 ord. Markdown med orienterande rubriker. Ingen kommersiell CTA.\\n\\n## Underlag\\n\\nKommunens pilotrapport Bokning av föreningslokaler, 8 april 2026, gäller ett åtta veckor långt försök i två lokaler. Under försöket gjordes 96 bokningar via webb och 24 via telefon. Rapporten räknar bokningar, inte unika personer. Den mäter inte ålder, funktionsförmåga eller digital vana. Telefonbokningarna kan därför inte användas för att säga hur stor andel av invånarna som inte kan boka digitalt.\\n\\nTjänsteutlåtandet inför kommunstyrelsens möte den 18 juni föreslår att telefonbokning tas bort för alla sju lokaler från september. Motivet i utlåtandet är att personalen ska slippa föra in uppgifter i två flöden. Någon tidsmätning eller ekonomisk besparingsberäkning finns inte i handlingarna. Förvaltningens verkliga invändning mot att behålla båda kanalerna är dubbel administration, inte att telefonanvändare är lata eller dyra.\\n\\nÖppna beslut föreslår ett sex månader långt försök i alla sju lokaler, med båda bokningsvägarna kvar. Under försöket ska förvaltningen registrera tidsåtgång per bokningsväg och be användarna frivilligt ange varför de väljer telefon eller webb. Föreningen gör inget anspråk på att ha finansierat eller kostnadsberäknat försöket. Kostnaden behöver kommunstyrelsen ta ställning till. Därefter kan den avgöra om en kanal ska tas bort, ändras eller behållas.\\n\\nSanna Eks ståndpunkt: ”Att en bokning går via telefon är inte ett bevis för att någon inte kan använda webben. Det är ett skäl att ta reda på varför telefonen fortfarande används.” Detta får citeras eller refereras. Hon menar att kommunen först måste kunna väga arbetskostnaden mot värdet för användarna. Hon motsätter sig inte digital bokning.\\n\\n## Boundaries / judging\\n\\nInga lagkrav, protester, diskrimineringsfall, partipolitiska motiv eller besparingar är belagda. Siffror ska tillskrivas rapporten där de bär argumentet. Författaren ska kunna kritisera beslutsunderlaget skarpt utan falska motiv eller tandlös neutralisering. Slutet ska namnge kommunstyrelsens möjliga beslut, med kostnadsosäkerheten bevarad.\\n\"}}}"}, {"type": "input_text", "text": "{\"file\":\"draft.md\",\"result\":{\"status\":\"fulfilled\",\"value\":{\"chunk_id\":\"0fb7ec\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000001708,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":653,\"output\":\"# Lervik should test before ending telephone bookings\\n\\nBooking totals in Lervik’s pilot report do not measure residents’ ability to book online. The municipal executive board must weigh staff costs against the value of telephone access, yet the documents contain neither a time measurement nor a calculation of financial savings.\\n\\nBy Sanna Ek, spokesperson for Öppna beslut\\n\\nThe administrative report for Lervik’s municipal executive board meeting on 18 June proposes removing telephone booking from all seven venues for associations from September. I do not oppose digital booking. But on behalf of Öppna beslut, I urge the board to postpone a permanent switch and test telephone and web booking together for six months.\\n\\n## Bookings tell us too little about users\\n\\nThe municipality’s pilot report, dated 8 April 2026, records 96 web bookings and 24 telephone bookings during an eight-week trial at two venues. Those are bookings, not unique people. The report measures neither age nor functional ability nor familiarity with digital technology.\\n\\nSo the telephone figure cannot tell us what proportion of Lervik’s residents cannot book online. Nor does choosing the telephone prove an inability to use the web. It gives us a reason to ask why that route is still used.\\n\\n## Staff time needs a fair comparison\\n\\nThe administration’s objection to keeping both routes is the administrative work across two channels: staff enter information in two workflows. That concern deserves to be taken seriously. But the documents provide no measurement of the time involved or calculation of financial savings.\\n\\nRemoving a route before establishing its value to users and the work it requires leaves the board without the comparison it needs. Administrative simplicity is a reason to investigate the balance, not a substitute for doing so.\\n\\n## A wider trial can inform the choice\\n\\nÖppna beslut proposes keeping both booking routes for six months at all seven venues. During that trial, the administration should record the time spent on each route and ask users to say voluntarily why they choose telephone or web booking.\\n\\nWe make no claim to have financed or costed that trial. The executive board must consider its cost. Asking for better evidence does not remove the need to decide what gathering it is worth.\\n\\n## Evidence should guide the final booking decision\\n\\nLervik’s municipal executive board should now postpone the permanent switch and agree a six-month trial of both routes, with its cost explicitly considered. After that, it can decide whether to remove, change or retain a booking channel.\\n\"}}}"}], "internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough": {"turn_id": "01a0f78b-98d1-7d21-8799-e3174dcc301b", "create_time": 1790859528.343077}}}
